Q12. The Dinesh Goswami Committee recommended
(a) the constitution of state-level election
commissions
(b) List System of election to the Lok Sabha
(c) governmental funding of parliamentary elections

(d) a ban on the candidature of independent
candidates in the parliamentary elections
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• The Dinesh Goswami Committee (1990) was
set up to suggest electoral reforms.
• It recommended partial state funding of
elections to reduce money power in politics.
• Suggested reforms included limiting the
number of constituencies, increasing security
deposits, and introducing Electronic Voting
Machines.
• Proposed proxy voting for personnel posted
abroad.
• Recommended a Model Code of Conduct and
speedy disposal of election disputes.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q13. Assertion (A): Willful disobedience or noncompliance of Court Orders and use of derogatory
language about judicial behaviour amounts to
Contempt of Court.
Reason (R): Judicial activism cannot be practised
without arming the judiciary with punitive powers to
punish contemptuous behaviour.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are true, but R is not a correct
explanation of A
(c) A is true, but R is false
(d) A is false, but R is true
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• Contempt of Court is defined under the
Contempt of Courts Act, 1971.
• Civil Contempt involves willful disobedience
to court orders.
• Criminal Contempt includes actions that
scandalize the court, prejudice judicial
proceedings, or obstruct justice.
• Judicial activism relies on the authority and
dignity of courts, which is protected by
contempt powers (Art. 129 & 215).
• R is true but it explains a broader principle,
not exactly why A is true.

Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1998
Q1. Which one of the following Schedules of the
Constitution of India contains provisions regarding
Anti-Defection Act?
(a) Second Schedule
(b) Fifth Schedule
(c) Eighth Schedule
(d) Tenth Schedule
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• The Tenth Schedule was inserted by the 52nd
Constitutional Amendment Act, 1985.
• It is popularly known as the Anti-Defection
Law.
• It lays down the grounds for disqualification
of MPs and MLAs on the grounds of
defection.
• Disqualification applies if a member
voluntarily gives up membership of the party
or votes/abstains contrary to party direction
without permission.
• Exceptions include mergers and splits (though
later the 91st Amendment removed
recognition of splits).
• The Presiding Officer (Speaker/Chairman)
decides disqualification cases.
• Judicial review of the Speaker’s decision was
upheld in Kihoto Hollohan case (1992).
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q2. The Indian parliamentary system is different
from the British parliamentary system in that India

has
(a) both a real and a nominal executive
(b) a system of collective responsibility
(c) bicameral legislature
(d) the system of judicial review
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Judicial review is a core feature of the Indian
Constitution, allowing courts to strike down
laws that violate the Constitution.
• Articles 13, 32, 131–136, 226, 245–246
empower courts to review legislative,
executive, and even judicial actions.
• In Britain, Parliament is supreme — no court
can declare a law unconstitutional.
• India, like the USA, has a written
Constitution, making judicial review essential
to maintain constitutional supremacy.
• This is a key distinction: the UK follows
parliamentary sovereignty, while India
follows constitutional supremacy.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 1999
Q1. The Constitution of India recognises
(a) only religious minorities
(b) only linguistic minorities
(c) religious and linguistic minorities
(d) religious, linguistic and ethnic minorities
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• The Constitution does not define “minority”,
but refers to religious and linguistic
minorities in various Articles.
• Article 29(1): Protects the right to conserve
distinct language, script or culture of any
section of citizens.
• Article 29(2): Prohibits denial of admission to
educational institutions on grounds of
religion, race, caste, language.
• Article 30(1): Gives minorities the right to
establish and administer educational
institutions of their choice.

•

Article 30(2): Prohibits discrimination in
granting aid to minority institutions.
• Articles 347, 350A, 350B: Provide for
linguistic protection, instruction in mother
tongue, and appointment of a Special Officer
for Linguistic Minorities.
• Sikh community’s right to wear and carry
Kirpans is also recognized (Art. 25).
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. In the new Panchayati Raj Bill enacted in 1993,
there are several fresh provisions deviating from the
past. Which one of the following is not one such
provision?
(a) A number of added responsibilities in the area of
agriculture, rural development, primary education and
social forestry among others.
(b) Elections being made mandatory for all posts at
the time they are due.
(c) A statutory representation for women in the
panchayats, upto a third of the strength.
(d) Regular remuneration to the panchayat members,
so as to ensure their punctuality and accountability
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• The 73rd Constitutional Amendment Act,
1992 gave constitutional status to PRIs.
• It introduced eleven functional areas
including agriculture, rural development,
primary education, social forestry, etc.
• Made regular elections compulsory even if
bodies are dissolved — within six months.
• Provided reservation of not less than onethird seats for women in all Panchayati Raj
bodies.
• There is no constitutional mandate for
regular remuneration to panchayat members.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. Consider the following statements: An
amendment of the Constitution of India can be
initiated by the
(1) Lok Sabha
(2) Rajya Sabha

(3) State Legislatures
(4) President
Which of the above statements is/are correct?
(a) 1 alone
(b) 1, 2 and 3
(c) 2, 3 and 4
(d) 1 and 2
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Article 368 lays down the procedure for
amending the Constitution.
• An amendment bill can be introduced in
either House of Parliament — Lok Sabha or
Rajya Sabha.
• It cannot be initiated by State Legislatures or
the President.
• The bill must be passed in each House by a
special majority:
o Majority of total membership, and
o Two-thirds majority of members
present and voting.
• For federal provisions, ratification by half of
State Legislatures is also required.
• The President must give assent once passed;
he cannot withhold or return it.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q4. Consider the following statements regarding the
National Human Rights Commission of India:
(1) Its Chairman must be a retired Chief Justice of
India.
(2) It has formations in each state as State Human
Rights Commission.
(3) Its powers are only recommendatory in nature.

(4) It is mandatory to appoint a woman as a member
of the Commission.
(a) 1, 2, 3 and 4
(b) 2 and 4
(c) 2 and 3
(d) 1 and 3
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• The NHRC was established in 1993 under the
Protection of Human Rights Act, 1993 (not
constitutional).
• Its Chairman must be a retired Chief Justice
of India.
• States may set up State Human Rights
Commissions, but they are not formations of
NHRC; they are separate statutory bodies.
• NHRC’s powers are recommendatory, not
binding — it can inquire and recommend but
not punish.
• There is no mandatory requirement to
appoint a woman member.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q5. A British citizen staying in India cannot claim
Right to
(a) Freedom of trade and profession
(b) Equality before the Law
(c) Protection of life and personal liberty
(d) Freedom of religion
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
Fundamental Rights only for citizens include:
• Protection from discrimination (Art. 15),
• Equality of opportunity in public employment
(Art. 16),
• Freedom of speech, assembly, association,
movement, residence, profession (Art. 19),
• Cultural & educational rights of minorities
(Art. 30).
• Rights available to all (citizens & foreigners)
include:
• Equality before law (Art. 14),
• Protection of life & liberty (Art. 21),
• Freedom of religion (Art. 25),

•

Rights against exploitation (Art. 23–24), etc.
• Hence, a British citizen in India cannot claim
Art. 19 rights, including freedom of
trade/profession.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2000
Q1. “….instil into the vast millions of workers, men
and women, who actually do the job, a sense of
partnership and of cooperative performance....” The
above passage relates to
(a) Planned Development
(b) Community Development
(c) Panchayati Raj System
(d) Integrated Development Programme
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• Launched on 2 Oct 1952, the Community
Development Programme (CDP) sought
people’s participation in rural development.
• Implemented via Community Development
Blocks (~100 villages, 400–500 sq km, 60–70k
population).
• Emphasised self-help, cooperation, and local
initiative rather than top-down delivery.
• Covered multiple sectors: agriculture,
irrigation, health, education, roads,
sanitation.
• Provided the administrative framework that
later supported Panchayati Raj expansion in
the 1960s.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. The Speaker can ask a member of the House to
stop speaking and let another member speak. This
phenomenon is known as
(a) decorum
(b) crossing the floor
(c) interpellation
(d) yielding the floor
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• “Yielding the floor”: the Chair/Speaker directs
a member to give way so another may speak.

•

Ensures orderly debate, time discipline, and
balanced participation among parties.
• Rule of Chair’s authority: members must
resume their seats when Speaker rises.
• Different from interpellation (formal question
to govt) and crossing the floor (changing
seating sides).
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. Consider the following statements about the
Attorney-General of India:
1. He is appointed by the President of India.
2. He must have the same qualifications as are
required for a Judge of the Supreme Court.
3. He must be a member of either House of
Parliament.
4. He can be removed by impeachment by
Parliament.
Which of these statements are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 1 and 3
(c) 2, 3 and 4
(d) 3 and 4
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• Art. 76: Appointed by the President; tenure
at President’s pleasure (no fixed term).
• Qualifications: eligible to be SC Judge (citizen
+ 5 yrs HC judge/10 yrs HC
advocate/distinguished jurist).
• Not required to be an MP; may participate
but not vote in Parliament/committees.
• No impeachment: removal is executive
pleasure, not a legislative process.
• Has right of audience in all courts and advises
Union Govt/President on legal matters.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. Consider the following functionaries:
1. Cabinet Secretary
2. Chief Election Commissioner
3. Union Cabinet Ministers
4. Chief Justice of India.
Their correct sequence, in the Order of Precedence,
is

(a) 3, 4, 2, 1
(b) 4, 3, 1, 2
(c) 4, 3, 2, 1
(d) 3, 4, 1, 2
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• Order of Precedence (protocol list): CJI ranks
above Union Cabinet Ministers (by seniority),
followed by CEC, then Cabinet Secretary.
• It is ceremonial, used for state functions, not
a line of succession or power hierarchy.
• Reflects constitutional importance and
protocol seating/priority at official events.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. The primary function of the Finance Commission
in India is to
(a) distribute revenue between the Centre and the
States
(b) prepare the Annual Budget
(c) advise the President on financial matters
(d) allocate funds to various ministries of the Union
and State Governments
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• Art. 280: Constituted every 5 years (or earlier)
to recommend tax devolution Union→States
and among States (vertical & horizontal).
• Recommends grants-in-aid under Art. 275,
fiscal equalization, and other referred
matters (e.g., disaster relief norms).
• Submits report to President; action taken
tabled in Parliament.
• Does not prepare the Union Budget or
allocate to ministries (that’s
MoF/Parliament).
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q6. The State which has the largest number of seats
reserved for the Scheduled Tribes in Lok Sabha is
(a) Bihar
(b) Gujarat
(c) Uttar Pradesh
(d) Madhya Pradesh
Answer: (d)

Explanation:
• Arts. 330–332: Reservation for SC/ST seats
proportional to population.
• As per the then delimitation: Madhya
Pradesh had the highest ST-reserved seats
among listed options (e.g., ~5), Gujarat ~4,
Bihar/UP—none.
• Subject to periodic readjustment post-census,
but then-current data favoured M.P.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q7. A college student desires to get elected to the
Municipal Council of his city. The validity of his
nomination would depend on the important
condition, among others, that
(a) he obtains permission from the Principal of his
college
(b) he is a member of a political party
(c) his name figures in the Voters’ List
(d) he files a declaration owing allegiance to the
Constitution of India
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• Urban local body eligibility includes:
citizenship, age ≥21, name on electoral roll of
the concerned ward, and no disqualification.
• Party membership/college permission are
not legal prerequisites.
• Reservation norms (SC/ST/OBC/Women) may
apply by seat; otherwise no separate
allegiance declaration needed beyond the
nomination affidavit.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q8. The Parliament can make any law for the whole
or any part of India for implementing International
treaties
(a) with the consent of all the States
(b) with the consent of the majority of States
(c) with the consent of the States concerned
(d) without the consent of any State
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Art. 253: Parliament may legislate for
whole/any part of India to implement

international treaties, agreements,
conventions, even on State List subjects.
• State consent not required; ensures India can
fulfil international obligations.
• Examples: UN Privileges & Immunities Act,
1947, Geneva Conventions Act, 1960, AntiHijacking Act, 1982.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q9. Which one of the following statements about a
Money Bill is not correct?
(a) A Money Bill can be tabled in either House of
Parliament
(b) The Speaker of Lok Sabha is the final authority to
decide whether a Bill is a Money Bill or not
(c) The Rajya Sabha must return a Money Bill passed
by Lok Sabha and send it for consideration within 14
days
(d) The President cannot return a Money Bill to Lok
Sabha for reconsideration
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• Art. 110: A Money Bill can be introduced only
in Lok Sabha, and only with President’s
recommendation.
• Speaker’s certification is final and cannot be
questioned in court (as a rule of internal
procedure).
• Rajya Sabha can recommend amendments
but must return within 14 days; Lok Sabha
may accept/reject.
• President cannot return a Money Bill; can
only assent or withhold.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q10. The 73rd Constitution Amendment Act, 1992
refers to the
(a) generation of gainful employment in rural areas
(b) employment during lean agricultural season
(c) laying the foundation for strong and vibrant
Panchayati Raj Institutions in the country
(d) guarantee of right to life, liberty, equality and
security
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• Inserted Part IX (Arts. 243–243-O) and
Eleventh Schedule (29 subjects).
• Provided 3-tier PRIs, regular elections, State
Election Commissions, State Finance
Commissions.
• Reservations: ≥1/3 seats & chairpersons for
women; SC/ST as per population.
• Objective: devolution, democratic
decentralization, local planning &
implementation.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q11. Which one of the following statements is
incorrect?
(a) Goa attained full statehood in 1987
(b) Diu is an island in the Gulf of Khambhat
(c) Daman and Diu were separated from Goa by the
56th Amendment of the Constitution of India
(d) Dadra and Nagar Haveli were under French
colonial rule till 1954
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Goa statehood on 30 May 1987 (correct).
• Diu is an island off the Gulf of Khambhat
(correct).
• 56th Amendment, 1987: granted Goa
statehood; Daman & Diu remained a UT
(correct).
• Dadra & Nagar Haveli were Portuguese (not
French); liberated 1954, integrated 1961 (so
(d) is incorrect).
Hence, option (d) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 2001
Q1. In what way does the Indian Parliament exercise
control over the administration?
(a) Through Consultative Committees of various
ministries
(b) Through Parliamentary Committees
(c) By making the administrators send periodic reports
(d) By compelling the executive to issue writs
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• Parliament in India exercises executive
accountability primarily through
Parliamentary Committees because House
time is limited and detailed examination of
every policy or budget is not feasible in the
main chamber.
• Standing Committees (e.g., Public Accounts
Committee (PAC), Estimates Committee,
Public Undertakings Committee, DRSCs) and
Ad hoc Committees are constituted for
detailed scrutiny of administrative actions.
• These Committees examine government
policies, budgetary allocations, performance,
policy implementation, and outcomes.
• They can summon officials, demand
documents, take evidence, and submit
recommendations. This ensures continuous
and informed oversight.
• Consultative Committees attached to
ministries are only advisory in nature, and do
not have oversight powers.
• Issuing writs is a judicial function, not a
parliamentary one.
• Parliament’s role is political and procedural,
focusing on ensuring that the executive
remains answerable for its decisions.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. The Speaker can ask a member of the House to
stop speaking and let another member speak. This
phenomenon is known as
(a) decorum
(b) crossing the floor
(c) interpellation

(d) yielding the floor
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• “Yielding the floor” is a parliamentary
practice where the Speaker directs a member
who is speaking to give way to another
member.
• This is done to maintain decorum, ensure
time management, and give opportunities to
multiple members to participate.
• It is used especially during heated debates,
Question Hour, or discussions on bills, where
structured participation is necessary.
• Interpellation is a formal parliamentary
question procedure common in some
European systems, not typically in Indian
parliamentary terminology.
• Crossing the floor means changing party
sides during voting or debate — a serious
matter under the Anti-Defection Law (Tenth
Schedule).
• Decorum is a general term for order and
discipline in the House.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. Consider the following statements regarding the
High Courts in India:
1. There are eighteen High Courts in the country.
2. Three of them have jurisdiction over more
than one State.
3. No Union Territory has a High Court of its
own.
4. Judges of the High Court hold office till the
age of 62.
Which of these statements is/are correct?
(a) 1, 2 and 4
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 4
(d) 4 only
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Article 217 of the Constitution specifies that
High Court Judges hold office until 62 years
of age.

•

The number of High Courts has increased
beyond 18 due to new States and UT
reorganisations (e.g., Telangana High Court in
2019). So statement 1 .
• Some High Courts have jurisdiction over more
than one State/UT, e.g.:
o Bombay High Court → Maharashtra,
Goa, and UTs of Daman & Diu and
Dadra & Nagar Haveli.
o Punjab & Haryana High Court →
jurisdiction over both states and
Chandigarh.
o Guwahati High Court → earlier had
multiple North-Eastern states.
Statement 2 is factually correct, but
the answer framing focuses on
elimination logic.
• Delhi, a Union Territory, has its own High
Court since 1966. Statement 3 .
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4. If a new State of the Indian Union is to be
created, which one of the following Schedules of the
Constitution must be amended?
(a) First
(b) Second
(c) Third
(d) Fifth
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• The First Schedule lists the names of States
and Union Territories, along with their
territorial extent.
• When a new State is created, merged, or
boundaries are altered, Parliament must
amend the First Schedule under Articles 2 &
3.
• This is done by a simple majority, not a
special majority (as it is not treated as a
constitutional amendment under Article 368).
• Second Schedule deals with salaries and
allowances of officials.
• Third Schedule relates to forms of oaths and
affirmations.

•

Fifth Schedule deals with administration of
Scheduled Areas and Tribes.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q5. Which Article of the Constitution provides that it
shall be the endeavour of every State to provide
adequate facility for instruction in the mother
tongue at the primary stage of education?
(a) Article 349
(b) Article 350
(c) Article 350-A
(d) Article 351
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• Article 350A was inserted by the Seventh
Amendment (1956).
• It places a directive obligation on States to
endeavour to provide mother tongue
instruction at the primary stage for children
belonging to linguistic minorities.
• The President may issue directions to ensure
compliance with this Article.
• Article 350 ensures citizens can submit
representations to authorities in any
language used in the Union or State.
• Article 349 deals with special procedure
regarding language (restriction on
Parliament’s power to make language-related
laws for 15 years).

•

Article 351 provides for promotion of Hindi as
a national language.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6. The Supreme Court of India tenders advice to the
President on a matter of law or fact
(a) on its own initiative
(b) only if he seeks such advice
(c) only if the matter relates to the Fundamental
Rights of citizens
(d) only if the issue poses a threat to the unity and
integrity of the country
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• Article 143 empowers the President to refer
questions of law or fact to the Supreme
Court for advisory opinion.
• The Supreme Court cannot give advice suo
motu (on its own). The President must
initiate the reference.
• The advisory jurisdiction is very wide — it is
not limited to Fundamental Rights or national
integrity issues.
• Although the Court’s opinion is not binding, it
carries great persuasive value. Examples
include references on the Ayodhya dispute
(1993) and Kerala Education Bill (1958).
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q7. Which one of the following duties is NOT
performed by the Comptroller and Auditor General
of India?
(a) To audit and report on all expenditure from the
Consolidated Fund of India
(b) To audit and report on all expenditure from the

Contingency Funds and Public Accounts
(c) To audit and report on all trading, manufacturing,
profit and loss accounts
(d) To control the receipt and issue of public money,
and to ensure that the public revenue is lodged in the
exchequer
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• CAG (Articles 148–151) is an independent
constitutional authority.
• He audits:
o Consolidated Fund of Union & States.
o Contingency Funds and Public
Accounts.
o Departmental commercial
undertakings and PSUs (as per law).
• The CAG’s function is post-facto audit → he
checks whether money was legally spent for
the intended purpose.
• Control over receipts and expenditure is
exercised by the executive/treasury, not CAG.
• This separation ensures checks and balances
between executive financial control and
independent audit.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q8. Which one of the following statements correctly
describes the Fourth Schedule of the Constitution of
India?
(a) It lists the distribution of power between the
Union and the States
(b) It contains the languages listed in the Constitution
(c) It contains the provisions regarding the
administration of tribal areas
(d) It allocates seats in the Council of States
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• The Fourth Schedule deals with allocation of
seats in the Rajya Sabha (Council of States) to
States and Union Territories.
• It ensures federal representation in the
Upper House based on population while
maintaining equality of status among States.
• Seventh Schedule → Union, State, Concurrent
Lists.

•

Eighth Schedule → Official languages.
Fifth and Sixth Schedules → administration of
Scheduled Areas and Tribes in different
regions.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
•

symbol reservation across states, and
consultation rights with the ECI.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 2002
Q9. Consider the following statements regarding the
political parties in India:
1. The Representation of the People Act, 1951
provides for the registration of political
parties.
2. Registration of political parties is carried out
by the Election Commission.
3. A national level political party is one which is
recognised in four or more States.
4. During the 1999 general elections, there were
six National and 48 State level parties
recognised by the Election Commission.
Which of these statements are correct?
(a) 1, 2 and 4
(b) 1 and 3
(c) 2 and 4
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Section 29A of the Representation of the
People Act, 1951 enables political parties to
register with the Election Commission of
India (ECI).
• The ECI registers parties and recognises them
as national or state parties under the Election
Symbols (Reservation and Allotment) Order,
1968.
• A National Party must be recognised in four
or more States and fulfill vote share or seat
criteria (e.g., 6% valid votes + 4 Lok Sabha
seats or recognition in 4 states).
• In the 1999 General Elections, there were 6
National Parties and 48 State Parties, per ECI
records.
• Registration gives parties certain legal
benefits, tax exemptions, and symbol
reservation rights, while recognition gives
them election privileges like free airtime,

Q1. The members of the Constituent Assembly which
drafted the Constitution of India were
(a) nominated by the British Parliament
(b) nominated by the Governor General
(c) elected by the Legislative Assemblies of various
provinces
(d) elected by the Indian National Congress and
Muslim League
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• The Cabinet Mission Plan of 1946 laid down
the procedure for setting up a Constituent
Assembly in India.
• The Provincial Legislative Assemblies (elected
under the 1935 Act in 1946 elections) were to
elect members to the Assembly by the
system of proportional representation by
means of the single transferable vote (STV).
• Seats were allocated in proportion to
population: 292 from provinces, 93 from
princely states, and 4 from Chief
Commissioner’s provinces, totaling 389
members.
• The princely states nominated their
representatives. There was no direct election
by the people, and neither the British
Parliament nor the Governor-General
nominated all members.
• After partition, the Assembly was
reconstituted with 299 members, who
drafted the Constitution over 2 years, 11
months, 18 days.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. Which one of the following rights was described
by Dr B R Ambedkar as the heart and soul of the
Constitution?

(a) Right to freedom of religion
(b) Right to property
(c) Right to equality
(d) Right to Constitutional remedies
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Article 32 guarantees the Right to
Constitutional Remedies, i.e., the right to
approach the Supreme Court for
enforcement of Fundamental Rights.
• The Supreme Court may issue five writs:
o Habeas corpus (produce the body)
o Mandamus (we command)
o Prohibition (stop proceedings)
o Certiorari (transfer/review of
proceedings)
o Quo warranto (by what authority)
• Dr. B.R. Ambedkar called Article 32 the “heart
and soul of the Constitution” because it gives
teeth to Fundamental Rights, making them
meaningful and enforceable.
• It is itself a Fundamental Right, unique among
constitutions.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q3. Which one of the following Articles of the
Directive Principles of State Policy deals with the
promotion of international peace and security?
(a) 51
(b) 48 A
(c) 43 A
(d) 41
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• Article 51 (Directive Principles, Part IV) directs
the State to:

o

Promote international peace and
security,
o Maintain just and honourable
relations between nations,
o Foster respect for international law
and treaty obligations, and
o Encourage settlement of
international disputes by arbitration.
• Article 51 reflects India’s commitment to
world peace, aligning with Panchsheel
principles and the UN Charter.
• Article 48A: protection and improvement of
the environment.
• Article 43A: workers’ participation in
management of industries.
• Article 41: right to work, education, and
public assistance.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4. With reference to Indian Polity, which one of the
following statements is correct?
(a) Planning Commission is accountable to Parliament
(b) President can make ordinance only when either of
the two Houses of Parliament is not in session
(c) The minimum age prescribed for appointment as a
Judge of the Supreme Court is 40 years
(d) National Development Council is constituted of
Union Finance Minister and the Chief Ministers of all
the States
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• Article 123 empowers the President to
promulgate ordinances when either House of
Parliament is not in session, and immediate
action is necessary.
• The ordinance has the same force as a law,
but must be laid before Parliament and
ceases to operate after six weeks of
reassembly if not approved.
• The Planning Commission (1950–2014) was
an extra-constitutional advisory body, not
accountable to Parliament.
• There is no minimum age for Supreme Court
Judges in Article 124; eligibility is based on 5

years as High Court judge, 10 years as High
Court advocate, or eminent jurist.
• National Development Council (NDC)
included the PM (Chairman), Union Cabinet
Ministers, Chief Ministers of all States, and
UT representatives—not just the FM and
CMs.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. Which one of the following authorities
recommends the principles governing the grants-inaid of the revenues to the states out of the
Consolidated Fund of India?
(a) Finance Commission
(b) Inter-State Council
(c) Union Ministry of Finance
(d) Public Accounts Committee
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• Article 280 provides for a Finance
Commission every five years to recommend:
o Distribution of tax proceeds between
Centre and States,
o Principles for grants-in-aid of States’
revenues from the Consolidated Fund
of India (Article 275),
o Measures to augment State finances
to support Panchayats and
Municipalities (after 73rd and 74th
Amendments).
• Inter-State Council (Art. 263) is a
coordination forum, not fiscal.
• Union Finance Ministry executes finances but
does not constitutionally recommend
principles.
• Public Accounts Committee scrutinises
expenditure after it occurs.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q6. In the Indian Constitution, the Right to Equality is
granted by Five Articles. They are
(a) Article 16 to Article 20
(b) Article 15 to Article 19
(c) Article 14 to Article 18

(d) Article 13 to Article 17
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• Articles 14 to 18 (Part III) guarantee Right to
Equality:
o Article 14: Equality before law and
equal protection of laws (based on
Dicey’s concept).
o Article 15: Prohibits discrimination on
grounds of religion, race, caste, sex,
or place of birth; allows protective
discrimination for women, children,
SC/ST, and SEBC.
o Article 16: Equal opportunity in
matters of public employment; allows
reservations.
o Article 17: Abolition of
untouchability and its practice in any
form.
o Article 18: Abolition of titles, except
military and academic distinctions.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. Which one of the following amendments to the
Indian Constitution empowers the President to send
back any matter for reconsideration by the Council of
Ministers?
(a) 39th
(b) 40th
(c) 42nd
(d) 44th
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• 42nd Amendment (1976) made the President
bound to act on the advice of the Council of
Ministers.
• 44th Amendment (1978) modified Article 74
to allow the President to once return the
advice for reconsideration.
• If the Council of Ministers reiterates its
advice, the President must act on it.
• This provision strengthens the President’s
position slightly, enabling deliberation
without disturbing parliamentary democracy.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q8. In the case of election to the Lok Sabha, the
amount of Security deposited for general category
candidates and SC/ST category candidates
respectively is
(a) Rs 5,000 and Rs 2,500
(b) Rs 10,000 and Rs 2,500
(c) Rs 10,000 and Rs 5,000
(d) Rs 15,000 and Rs 7,500
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• Under the Representation of the People Act,
1951, candidates contesting Lok Sabha
elections must deposit:
o ₹10,000 for General category,
o ₹5,000 for SC/ST candidates.
• The deposit is forfeited if the candidate fails
to secure more than 1/6th (16.67%) of valid
votes polled in the constituency.
• This provision aims to discourage non-serious
candidates.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

(b) free and compulsory education for all children
between the age of 6 and 14 years
(c) reservation of 30 per cent posts for women in
government recruitments
(d) allocation of more number of parliamentary seats
for recently created States
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• The 93rd Constitutional Amendment Bill
(2001) inserted Article 21-A, making free and
compulsory education for children aged 6–14
years a Fundamental Right.
• It was later enacted as the 86th
Constitutional Amendment Act, 2002.
• The Right of Children to Free and Compulsory
Education Act (RTE), 2009 operationalized it
from 1 April 2010.
• This amendment shifted education for 6–14
age group from DPSPs to Fundamental
Rights, a landmark in social legislation.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q9. The term of the Lok Sabha
(a) cannot be extended under any circumstances
(b) can be extended by six months at a time
(c) can be extended by one year at a time during the
proclamation of emergency
(d) can be extended for two years at a time during the
proclamation of emergency
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• Article 83(2): Normal term of the Lok Sabha is
5 years from its first sitting.
• During a National Emergency (Article 352),
Parliament can extend the term of the Lok
Sabha by one year at a time, and not beyond
6 months after the Emergency ends.
• This was done during the Emergency (1975–
77).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 2003

Q10. The 93rd Constitutional Amendment Bill deals
with the
(a) continuation of reservation for backward classes in
government employment

Q1. Which one of the following statements is
correct?
(a) Only the Rajya Sabha and not the Lok Sabha can
have nominated members
(b) There is a constitutional provision for nominating
two members belonging to the Anglo-Indian
community to the Rajya Sabha
(c) There is no constitutional bar for a nominated
member to be appointed as a Union minister.
(d) A nominated member can vote both in the
Presidential and Vice-Presidential elections
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• Article 75 does not bar a nominated MP from
being appointed as a Union Minister. Any
MP—elected or nominated—can be
appointed to the Council of Ministers.
• Lok Sabha: Article 331 allowed the President
to nominate up to two Anglo-Indians if not
adequately represented. (This provision was

abolished by the 104th Amendment Act,
2019).
• Rajya Sabha: Article 80(1)(a) allows the
President to nominate 12 members with
special knowledge in literature, science, art or
social service. There was no Anglo-Indian
quota here.
• Nominated MPs:
o Cannot vote in Presidential elections
(Article 54 explanation).
o Can vote in Vice-Presidential
elections (Article 66).
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. Who has the power to enlarge the jurisdiction of
the Supreme Court of India?
(a) The President of India
(b) The Chief Justice of India
(c) The Parliament
(d) The Union Ministry of Law, Justice and Company
Affairs
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• Article 138(1): Parliament may by law confer
further jurisdiction and powers on the
Supreme Court with respect to any matter in
the Union List.
• Article 138(2): Parliament may by law enable
the Government of India and a State
Government to refer disputes to the Supreme
Court for advisory/jurisdictional settlement.
• The President has advisory powers under
Article 143, but cannot enlarge jurisdiction
unilaterally.
• CJI or Law Ministry have no such
constitutional authority.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Which one of the following High Courts has the
territorial jurisdiction over Andaman and Nicobar
Islands?
(a) Andhra Pradesh
(b) Calcutta
(c) Madras

(d) Orissa
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• Calcutta High Court, established in 1862, has
territorial jurisdiction over the State of West
Bengal and the Union Territory of Andaman
and Nicobar Islands.
• This jurisdiction was conferred to facilitate
administrative convenience during the
colonial period and continues postindependence.
• Madras HC covers Tamil Nadu & Puducherry;
Andhra HC covers Andhra Pradesh; Orissa HC
covers Odisha only.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. Which one of the following Schedules of the
Indian Constitution lists the names of States and
specifies their territories?
(a) First
(b) Second
(c) Third
(d) Fourth
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• First Schedule lists the names of States and
Union Territories, and specifies their
territorial extent.
• Any creation, merger, or boundary change of
States under Articles 2 & 3 requires
amendment of the First Schedule through an
ordinary law.
• Second Schedule → Salaries, emoluments of
constitutional authorities.
• Third Schedule → Forms of oaths and
affirmations.
• Fourth Schedule → Allocation of seats in the
Rajya Sabha.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q5. The Ninth Schedule to the Indian Constitution
was added by:
(a) First Amendment
(b) Eighth Amendment
(c) Ninth Amendment

(d) Forty-second Amendment
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• First Amendment Act, 1951 inserted Article
31B and the Ninth Schedule.
• Purpose: to protect land reform and agrarian
legislation from being challenged in courts on
the ground of violating Fundamental Rights,
especially right to property.
• Article 31B provides that none of the Acts
included in the Ninth Schedule shall be
deemed void for being inconsistent with Part
III.
• After Kesavananda Bharati (1973), laws
inserted into the Ninth Schedule after 24 April
1973 are open to judicial review if they
violate the basic structure of the Constitution
(I.R. Coelho case, 2007).
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q6. The second joint sitting of the two Houses of
Indian Parliament was held to pass the Banking
Service Commission (Repeal) Bill. Which of these
statements is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Article 108 allows joint sittings of both
Houses in case of deadlock over an ordinary
bill, not Money/CA bills.
• There have been only three joint sittings in
history:
1. Dowry Prohibition Bill (1961) – First
joint sitting.
2. Banking Service Commission (Repeal)
Bill (1978) – Second joint sitting.
3. Prevention of Terrorism Bill (POTA)
(2002) – Third joint sitting.
• All statements referring to these instances are
correct.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q7. Under which Article of the Indian Constitution
did the President give his assent to the ordinance on
electoral reforms when it was sent back to him by
the Union Cabinet without making any changes (in
2002)?
(a) Article 121
(b) Article 122
(c) Article 123
(d) Article 124
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• Article 123 empowers the President to
promulgate ordinances when Parliament is
not in session and immediate action is
required.
• An ordinance has the same force as an Act,
but must be laid before Parliament and
approved within 6 weeks of reassembly.
• In 2002, the President returned the Electoral
Reforms Ordinance for reconsideration. The
Cabinet sent it back unchanged, and the
President was constitutionally bound to
promulgate it.
• Articles 121 & 122 deal with Parliamentary
proceedings, not ordinances; 124 deals with
SC judges.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q8. Which one of the following Articles of the Indian
Constitution provides that “It shall be the duty of the
Union to protect every State against external
aggression and internal disturbance”?
(a) Article 215
(b) Article 275
(c) Article 325

(d) Article 355
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Article 355: It is the duty of the Union to
protect every State against external
aggression and internal disturbance, and to
ensure that the Government of every State is
carried on in accordance with the
Constitution.
• This Article forms the constitutional basis for
Central intervention, including President’s
Rule under Article 356 in grave situations.
• It also provides the foundation for
deployment of central forces to aid states.
• Articles 215, 275, 325 deal with HCs, grants,
and elections respectively.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q9. Which one of the following Bills must be passed
by each House of the Indian Parliament separately,
by special majority?
(a) Money Bill
(b) Finance Bill
(c) Ordinary Bill
(d) Constitution Amendment Bill
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Article 368 lays down the procedure for
Constitutional Amendment Bills.
• They must be passed in each House
separately by a special majority:
o Majority of the total membership of
the House, and
o 2/3 of the members present and
voting.
• There is no provision for a joint sitting for
Constitution Amendment Bills.
• Certain amendments (e.g., affecting federal
provisions like Articles 54, 73, 162) also
require ratification by ½ of the State
Legislatures.
• Money Bills (Article 110) can only be
introduced in Lok Sabha, and Rajya Sabha has
limited powers.

•

Finance Bill includes financial provisions but
follows ordinary or money bill procedures
depending on type.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2004
Q1. With reference to Indian Public Finance, consider
the following statements:
1. Disbursements from Public Accounts of India
are subject to the Vote of Parliament.
2. The Indian Constitution provides for the
establishment of a Consolidated Fund, a
Public Account and a Contingency Fund for
each State.
3. Appropriations and disbursements under the
Railway Budget are subject to the same form
of parliamentary control as other
appropriations and disbursements.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• Public Account (Art. 266(2)) includes
transactions like provident funds, small
savings, etc. Parliamentary vote is not
required for withdrawals from this account.
• Constitution provides three funds —
Consolidated Fund, Public Account, and
Contingency Fund — separately for Union
and each State.
• Railway Budget is presented separately but
receipts and expenditures are part of the
Consolidated Fund.
• Parliamentary control over Railway Budget
appropriations is the same as General
Budget, ensuring accountability.
• Statement 1 is incorrect (no vote for Public
Account), 2 and 3 are correct.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. Consider the following statements:
1. The highest criminal court of the district is the
Court of District and Sessions Judge.
2. The District Judges are appointed by the
Governor in consultation with the High
Courts.
3. A person to be eligible for appointment as a
District Judge should be an advocate or a
pleader of seven years standing or more, or
an officer in judicial service of the Union or
the State.
4. When the Sessions Judge awards death
sentence, it must be confirmed by the High
Court before it is carried out.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2, 3 and 4
(c) 3 and 4
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• District & Sessions Judge is the highest civil
and criminal judicial authority at the district
level.
• Appointment is made by the Governor in
consultation with the High Court, ensuring
judicial independence.
• Eligibility: must be an advocate or pleader
with 7+ years’ standing or a judicial officer in
service.
• Death sentences awarded by Sessions Judge
require confirmation by the High Court even
without appeal.
• All four statements reflect the structure of
district judiciary under the Constitution &
CPC/CrPC.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. Consider the following statements:
1. The Speaker of Lok Sabha has the power to
adjourn the House sine die but, on
prorogation, it is only the President who can
summon the House.
2. Unless sooner dissolved or there is an
extension of the term, there is an automatic

dissolution of the Lok Sabha by efflux of time,
at the end of the period of five years, even if
no formal order of dissolution is issued by the
President.
3. The Speaker of Lok Sabha continues in office
even after the dissolution of the House and
until ‘immediately before the first meeting of
the House’.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Adjournment sine die = ending a sitting
indefinitely; this power lies with the Speaker.
• Prorogation ends a session, and President has
the power to summon or prorogue the House
under Article 85.
• Automatic dissolution occurs after 5 years,
even without a formal order, unless extended
during emergency.
• Speaker remains in office till just before the
first sitting of the new Lok Sabha — ensures
continuity.
• All three statements correctly reflect
parliamentary procedure and constitutional
practice.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4. Which one of the following statements is not
correct?
(a) In Lok Sabha, a no-confidence motion has to set
out the grounds on which it is based
(b) In the case of a no-confidence motion in Lok
Sabha, no conditions of admissibility have been laid
down in the Rules
(c) A motion of no-confidence, once admitted, has to
be taken up within ten days of the leave being granted
(d) Rajya Sabha is not empowered to entertain a
motion of no-confidence
Answer: (a)
Explanation:

•

•

•

•

•

No-confidence motion (Rule 198, Lok Sabha
Rules) expresses lack of confidence in the
Council of Ministers as a whole.
No grounds are required to be mentioned;
even if mentioned, they do not form part of
the motion.
If 50 or more members support the motion,
the Speaker admits it, and it must be taken up
within 10 days.
Rajya Sabha cannot move or pass noconfidence motions, as executive is
responsible only to Lok Sabha.
Statement (a) is factually incorrect, hence the
correct answer.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q5. Which one of the following statements correctly
describes the Fourth Schedule of the Constitution of
India?
(a) It contains the scheme of the distribution of
powers between the Union and the States
(b) It contains the languages listed in the Constitution
(c) It contains the provisions regarding the
administration of tribal areas
(d) It allocates seats in the Council of States
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Fourth Schedule deals with allocation of
seats in the Rajya Sabha (Council of States) to
each State and Union Territory.
• Allocation is based on population, ensuring
proportional representation of States in the
Upper House.
• It has been amended from time to time due
to State reorganisation and formation of new
States.
• It helps maintain federal balance by
determining representation in the Rajya
Sabha.
• Other schedules (like 7th & 8th) deal with
powers and languages.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q6. Consider the following tasks:
1. Superintendence, direction and conduct of
free and fair elections.
2. Preparation of electoral rolls for all elections
to the Parliament, State Legislatures and the
Office of the President and the Vice-President.
3. Giving recognition to political parties and
allotting election symbols to political parties
and individuals contesting the election.
4. Proclamation of final verdict in the case of
election disputes.
Which of the above are the functions of the Election
Commission of India?
(a) 1, 2 and 3
(b) 2, 3 and 4
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 4
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• Article 324 vests the superintendence,
direction and control of elections in the
Election Commission of India.
• Functions include conduct of elections to
Parliament, State Legislatures, President,
Vice-President.
• It prepares electoral rolls, recognises political
parties, and allots symbols.
• Election disputes are decided by courts, not
the Election Commission.
• So, 1, 2, and 3 are functions; 4 is not.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q7. The resolution for removing the Vice-President
of India can be moved in the:
(a) Lok Sabha alone
(b) Either House of Parliament
(c) Joint Sitting of Parliament
(d) Rajya Sabha alone
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Article 67(b) provides for removal of the VicePresident by a resolution of the Rajya Sabha,
passed by effective majority.
• Lok Sabha must agree by simple majority, but
the motion originates only in Rajya Sabha.
• 14 days’ advance notice must be given for
such a resolution.
• There is no impeachment procedure, unlike
for the President.
• Deputy Chairman of Rajya Sabha discharges
functions in case of vacancy.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q8. With reference to the Constitution of India,
which one of the following pairs is not correctly
matched?
(a) Forests — Concurrent List
(b) Stock Exchanges — State List
(c) Post Office Savings Bank — Union List
(d) Public Health — State List
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• Seventh Schedule distributes powers
between Union, State, and Concurrent Lists.
• Stock Exchanges and Futures Markets are
mentioned in Union List – Entry 48, not in the
State List.
• Forests were transferred from State List to
Concurrent List by the 42nd Amendment,
1976.
• Public Health is under the State List, Entry 6.
• Post Office Savings Bank is under Union List,
Entry 39.
• Hence, the mismatch is in (b).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q9. Consider the following statements:

1. The highest deciding body for planning in
India is the Planning Commission of India.
2. The Secretary of the Planning Commission of
India is also the Secretary of National
Development Council.
3. The Constitution includes economic and social
planning in the Concurrent List in the Seventh
Schedule of the Constitution of India.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 2 only
(d) 3 only
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• National Development Council (NDC), set up
in 1952, is the highest policy-making body on
planning (apart from Parliament), not the
Planning Commission.
• Secretary of Planning Commission also acts
as Secretary of the NDC, ensuring
coordination.
• Economic and Social Planning is listed in
Concurrent List – Item 20.
• Planning Commission (1950–2014) was
executive & advisory, not constitutional.
• NITI Aayog has replaced the Planning
Commission since 2015.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q10. With reference to Indian Parliament, which one
of the following is not correct?
(a) The Appropriation Bill must be passed by both the
Houses of Parliament before it can be enacted into
law
(b) No money shall be withdrawn from the
Consolidated Fund of India except under the
appropriation made by the Appropriation Act
(c) Finance Bill is required for proposing new taxes but
no other Bill/Act is required for making changes in the
rates of taxes which are already under operation
(d) No Money Bill can be introduced except on the
recommendation of the President
Answer: (c)
Explanation:

•

•

•
•

•
•

Finance Bill is required for proposing new
taxes and also for changes in existing tax
rates.
Appropriation Bill authorises withdrawal
from Consolidated Fund of India; must pass
both Houses.
Article 265: no tax can be levied or collected
except by authority of law.
Article 110 & Rules of Procedure govern
Finance Bills, which must be enacted within
75 days.
President’s recommendation is mandatory
for introduction of Money Bills.
(c) is factually incorrect, hence the answer.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q11. Which one of the following Articles of the
Constitution of India says that the executive power
of every State shall be so exercised as not to impede
or prejudice the exercise of the executive power of
the Union?
(a) Article 257
(b) Article 258
(c) Article 355
(d) Article 356
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• Article 257: The executive power of every
State shall be exercised not to impede or
prejudice the exercise of the executive power
of the Union.
• It allows the Union to give directions to
States for ensuring smooth functioning.
• It covers construction & maintenance of
means of communication, protection of
railways, and other matters of national
importance.
• Article 258 deals with delegation of Union
functions to States.
• Articles 355 and 356 relate to Union’s duty to
protect States and President’s Rule.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q12. Which one of the following Articles of the
Constitution of India prohibits the employment of
children in factories?
(a) Article 24
(b) Article 45
(c) Article 330
(d) Article 368
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• Article 24: Prohibits employment of children
below 14 years in factories, mines, or any
hazardous employment.
• It is part of the Fundamental Rights (Article
12–35), making it enforceable by law.
• Violation attracts penal provisions under the
Child Labour (Prohibition and Regulation)
Act, 1986.
• Article 45 relates to free and compulsory
education, Article 330 to reservation of seats,
Article 368 to constitutional amendments.
• This provision ensures protection of children
and aligns with Directive Principles and
international conventions.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q13. According to the National Human Rights
Commission Act, 1993, who amongst the following
can be its Chairman?
(a) Any serving Judge of the Supreme Court
(b) Any serving Judge of the High Court
(c) Only a retired Chief Justice of India
(d) Only a retired Chief Justice of High Court
Answer: (c)
Explanation:

•

•
•

•

•

National Human Rights Commission (NHRC)
was established in 1993 under the Protection
of Human Rights Act, 1993.
The Chairman must be a retired Chief Justice
of India.
It also has four ex-officio members — heads
of National Commissions for SCs, STs,
Minorities, and Women.
Members are appointed by the President
based on recommendations of a committee
including PM, Speaker, Home Minister, etc.
Tenure is 5 years or up to age 70, whichever
is earlier; not eligible for further govt
employment.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q14. Who among the following was never the Lok
Sabha Speaker?
(a) K.V.K. Sundaram
(b) G.S. Dhillon
(c) Baliram Bhagat
(d) Hukum Singh
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• K.V.K. Sundaram — Not a Speaker; he was
India’s first Law Secretary and 2nd Chief
Election Commissioner (1958–67).
• G.S. Dhillon — Speaker of 4th & 5th Lok
Sabha (1969–75).
• Baliram Bhagat — Speaker of 5th Lok Sabha
(1976–77).
• Hukum Singh — Speaker from 1962–67;
admitted the first No-Confidence Motion.
• This question tests factual recall of Lok Sabha
Speakers’ list.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q15. Consider the following events:
1. Fourth general elections in India
2. Formation of Haryana State
3. Mysore named as Karnataka State
4. Meghalaya and Tripura become full States
Which one of the following is the correct
chronological order of the above?
(a) 2 – 1 – 4 – 3

(b) 4 – 3 – 2 – 1
(c) 2 – 3 – 4 – 1
(d) 4 – 1 – 2 – 3
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• Haryana Formation (1966) – carved out of
Punjab on 1 November 1966.
• Fourth General Elections (1967) – held in
February 1967; marked political shifts.
• Meghalaya & Tripura Statehood (1972) –
became full-fledged states on 21 January
1972.
• Mysore renamed Karnataka (1973) – officially
renamed in 1973.
• This sequence reflects post-independence
state reorganization and political
development chronology.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2005
Q1) Consider the following statements:
1. There are 25 High Courts in India
2. Punjab, Haryana and the Union Territory of
Chandigarh have a common High Court.
3. National Capital Territory of Delhi has a High
Court of its own.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 2 and 3
(b) 1 and 2
(c) 1, 2 and 3
(d) 3 only
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• There are 24 High Courts in India (not 25).
• Punjab, Haryana & Chandigarh share a
common High Court at Chandigarh.
• Delhi High Court was set up in 1966 under the
Delhi High Court Act, 1966.
• The Punjab High Court was renamed as
Punjab and Haryana High Court after
reorganization in 1966.
• The principal seat of this common High Court
remains at Chandigarh.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q2) Consider the following statements:
1. The Parliament cannot enlarge the jurisdiction
of the Supreme Court of India as its
jurisdiction is limited to that conferred by the
Constitution.
2. The officers and servants of the Supreme
Court and High Courts are appointed by the
concerned Chief Justice and the
administrative expenses are charged on the
Consolidated Fund of India.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• Parliament can enlarge the jurisdiction of the
Supreme Court by law — hence statement 1 is
incorrect.
• Article 146: Appointments of officers and
servants of SC are made by the CJI, and
expenses are charged on Consolidated Fund
of India.
• Parliament can confer further powers on SC
regarding appeals from High Courts.
• Administrative control and expenditure
ensure independence of judiciary.
• This arrangement upholds separation of
powers.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3) Consider the following statements:
1. Article 301 pertains to the Right to Property.
2. Right to Property is a legal right but not a
Fundamental Right.
3. Article 300A was inserted in the Constitution
of India by the Congress Government at the
Centre by the 44th Constitutional
Amendment.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 2 only
(b) 2 and 3

(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• Article 301 deals with freedom of trade and
commerce, not property.
• Right to Property was removed from
Fundamental Rights and made a legal right
under Article 300A.
• 44th Amendment Act (1978) inserted Article
300A; this was done by the Janata
Government, not Congress.
• The Supreme Court in Jilubhai Nanbhai
Khachar v. State of Gujarat clarified Article
300A’s limited scope.
• Right to property is no longer part of the
basic structure.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q4) Consider the following statements:
1. Article 371A to 371I were inserted in the
Constitution of India to meet regional
demands of Nagaland, Assam, Manipur,
Andhra Pradesh, Sikkim, Mizoram, Arunachal
Pradesh and Goa.
2. Constitution of India and the United States of
America can envisage a dual policy (The Union
and the States) but a single citizenship.
3. A naturalized citizen of India can never be
deprived of his citizenship.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1, 2 and 3
(b) 1 and 3
(c) 3 only
(d) 1 only
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Articles 371 to 371J provide special
provisions for 11 states to address regional,
tribal and administrative needs.
• India has single citizenship; USA has dual
citizenship concept — so statement 2 is
incorrect.

•

•

•

A naturalized citizen can be deprived of
citizenship under Section 9(1) of Citizenship
Act, 1955 — statement 3 is incorrect.
These provisions protect local interests and
autonomy, especially in North-East and
backward regions.
The special provisions were added through
subsequent amendments, not originally in
Constitution.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q5) Consider the following statements:
1. Part IX of the Constitution of India contains
provisions for Panchayats and was inserted by
the Constitution (73rd Amendment) Act,
1992.
2. Part IXA of the Constitution of India contains
provisions for municipalities and the Article
243Q envisages two types of municipalities –
Municipal Council and a Municipal
Corporation for every state.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• Part IX (Articles 243–243O) was inserted by
73rd Amendment, 1992 – deals with
Panchayati Raj.
• Part IXA (Articles 243P–243ZG) by 74th
Amendment, 1992 – deals with
Municipalities.
• Article 243Q provides for three types of
municipalities: Nagar Panchayat, Municipal
Council, and Municipal Corporation — not just
two.
• Panchayati Raj established three-tier system
for local governance.
• Municipalities are institutions of selfgovernment in urban areas.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q6) Consider the following:
1. Disputes with mobile cellular companies
2. Motor accident cases
3. Pension cases
For which of the above are Lok Adalats held?
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2
(c) 2 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• Lok Adalats are provided under Legal Services
Authorities Act, 1987, implementing Article
39A (free legal aid).
• Most common cases: motor accident claims,
bank recovery suits, traffic challans, simple
criminal cases.
• They are not suitable for complex disputes
like telecom regulatory issues or pension
calculation disputes.
• First Lok Adalat was held in 1982 in Gujarat.
• Settlements in Lok Adalat are final and
binding.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7) Consider the following statements:
1. The Constitution of India has 20 parts.
2. There are 390 Articles in the Constitution of
India in all.
3. Ninth, Tenth, Eleventh and Twelfth schedules
were added to the Constitution of India by the
Constitution (Amendments) Acts.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 only
(c) 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• The Constitution currently has 25 Parts and
448 Articles.
• Initially, it had 22 Parts and 395 Articles; later
amended several times.
• Ninth Schedule (1st Amendment, 1951) –
land reforms, immune from judicial review.

•
•
•

Tenth (52nd Amendment, 1985) – Antidefection law.
Eleventh (73rd Amendment, 1992) –
Panchayats.
Twelfth (74th Amendment, 1992) –
Municipalities.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q8) Who among the following was the Chairman of
the Union Constitution Committee of the
Constitution Assembly?
(a) B. R. Ambedkar
(b) J. B. Kripalani
(c) Jawaharlal Nehru
(d) Alladi Krishnaswami Ayyar
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• Jawaharlal Nehru chaired the Union
Constitution Committee, Union Powers
Committee, and States Committee.
• The Constituent Assembly was formed in 1946
under the Cabinet Mission Plan.
• It took 2 years, 11 months, 17 days to draft
the Constitution.
• Total sittings: 11 sessions, 165 days (114 on
Draft Constitution).
• Nehru’s committees shaped federal structure
and division of powers.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q9) The Constitution (98th Amendment) Act is
related to:
(a) Empowering the Centre to levy and appropriate
service tax
(b) The Constitution of the National Judicial
Commission
(c) Readjustment of electoral constituencies on the
basis of the Population Census 2001
(d) The demarcation of new boundaries between
States
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• 98th Amendment Bill proposed establishing a
National Judicial Commission (NJC).

•

•
•

•

NJC was to handle appointment, transfer,
code of ethics, and inquiry against higher
judiciary judges.
It sought to insert Chapter IV-A in Part V of
the Constitution.
The Bill lapsed and was not enacted, but later
similar provisions appeared in 99th
Amendment (NJAC), which was struck down
in 2015.
This was an attempt to reform judicial
appointments process.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2006

Q1) Which one of the following subjects is under the
Union List in the Seventh Schedule of the
Constitution of India?
(a) Regulation of labour and safety in mines and
oilfields
(b) Agriculture
(c) Fisheries
(d) Public health
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• Union List includes regulation of mines &
mineral development and oilfields/mineral
oils (Entries 53–54).
• Labour and safety in mines/oilfields is a
Union subject due to national
importance/standards.
• Agriculture, public health, fisheries (inland)
are primarily State List subjects.
• Central control ensures uniform safety
standards in hazardous sectors.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q2) Consider the following statements:
1. There is no provision in the Constitution of
India to encourage equal pay for equal work
for both men and women.
2. The constitution of India does not define
backward classes.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only

(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (b)
Explanation:
• Article 39(d) (DPSP) directs equal pay for
equal work → statement 1 false.
• The Constitution does not define “backward
classes” (Art. 15(4), 16(4) permit special
provisions) → statement 2 true.
• Identification is via Commission/criteria, not a
constitutional definition.
• Courts have evolved tests (e.g., social &
educational backwardness).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3) Consider the following statements:
1. The Rajya Sabha alone has the power to
declare that it would be in national interest
for the Parliament to legislate with respect to
a matter in the State List.
2. Resolution approving the Proclamation of
Emergency are passed only by the Lok Sabha.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (a)
Explanation:
• Article 249: Rajya Sabha alone (2/3rd present
& voting) can empower Parliament to legislate
on State List in national interest.
• Emergency (Art. 352) approval requires both
Houses, not just Lok Sabha → statement 2
false.
• During Emergency, Parliament can legislate on
State List (Art. 250).
• RS’s special role reflects its federal character
(States’ House).
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q4) What does the 104th Constitution Amendment
Bill relate to?
(a) Abolition of Legislative Councils in certain States
(b) Introduction of dual citizenship for persons of
Indian origin living outside India
(c) Providing quota to socially and educationally
backward classes in private educational institutions.
(d) Providing quota for religious minorities in the
services under the Central Government.
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• The move paralleled the 93rd Amendment
(2005/2006) inserting Article 15(5).
• Enables reservation for SEBC/SC/ST in private
unaided educational institutions (nonminority).
• Seeks access & equity in higher/technical
education.
• Minority institutions remain protected under
Art. 30.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5) Which one among the following commissions
was set up in pursuance of a definite provision under
an Article of the Constitution of India?
(a) University Grants Commission
(b) National Human Rights Commission
(c) Election Commission
(d) Central Vigilance Commission
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• Election Commission is a constitutional body
under Article 324.
• UGC (UGC Act), NHRC (PHRA, 1993), CVC (CVC
Act) are statutory bodies.
• EC has superintendence, direction & control
of elections.
• Ensures independence via constitutional
safeguards.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6) Consider the following statements:
1. A person who has held office as a permanent
Judge of a High Court cannot plead or act in

any court or before any authority in India
except the Supreme Court.
2. A person is not qualified for appointment as a
Judge of a High Court in India unless he has
for at least five years held a judicial office in
the territory of India.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Article 220: Retired HC judge cannot practice
in any court or authority except SC and other
High Courts (not just SC) → statement 1
incorrect.
• Article 217(2): Qualification = 10 years judicial
office or 10 years advocate of HC; not 5 years
→ statement 2 incorrect.
• Must be a citizen of India.
• Appointments by President in consultation
(now via Collegium practice).
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q7) Consider the following statements:
1. Free and compulsory education to the
children of 6–14 years age group by the state
was made a Fundamental Right by the 76th
Amendment to the Constitution of India.
2. Sarva Shiksha Abhiyan seeks to provide
computer education even in rural areas.
3. Education was included in the Concurrent List
by the 42nd Amendment, 1976 to the
Constitution of India.
Which of the statements given above are correct?
(a) 1, 2 and 3
(b) 1 and 2, only
(c) 2 and 3, only
(d) 1 and 3, only
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• Fundamental Right to education (6–14) via
86th Amendment (2002) inserting Article 21A → statement 1 false.

•
•
•

SSA explicitly targets computer education to
bridge digital divide → statement 2 true.
Education moved to Concurrent List by 42nd
Amendment (1976) → statement 3 true.
RTE Act 2009 operationalized Art. 21-A (from
2010).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q8) Who is the President of the Council of Scientific
and Industrial Research?
(a) President of India
(b) Vice-President of India
(c) Prime Minister of India
(d) Union Minister of Science and Technology
Answer: (c)
Explanation:
• CSIR (1942) is an autonomous premier R&D
body.
• Its President is the Prime Minister of India.
• Vice President is typically the Science &
Technology Minister (ex officio roles).
• Pan-India labs across diverse S&T domains.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q9) Who was the Chief Justice of India when public
interest litigation (PIL) was introduced to the Indian
Judicial System?
(a) M. Hidayatullah
(b) A. M. Ahmadi
(c) A. S. Anand
(d) P. N. Bhagwati
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• PIL evolved in the late 1970s–1980s as
epistolary jurisdiction.
• Justice P. N. Bhagwati (along with Justice V.
R. Krishna Iyer) pioneered PIL expansion.
• Allowed locus standi relaxation for access to
justice for marginalized groups.
• Enabled taking letters/news reports as writ
petitions.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q10) Assertion (A): In India, every state has a High
Court in its territory.
Reason (R): The Constitution of India provides for a
High Court in each state.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not the
correct explanation of A.
(c) A is True but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
Answer: (d)
Explanation:
• Article 214: “There shall be a High Court for
each State.” → R is true.
• But some states share a common High Court
(e.g., Punjab & Haryana & UT Chandigarh),
and it may not be located in every state’s
territory → A is false.
• Seventh Amendment (1956) allows common
High Courts.
• India has 24 High Courts with combined
jurisdictions.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q1) Consider the following statements in respect of

1. A Proclamation of financial emergency issued

of both houses of Parliament.

in operation, it is competent for the president
of India to issue directions for the reduction of

of the Union but excluding the Judges of the

(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2

Q8) Which one of the following is the correct
