UPSC PYQs - 2009
Q1) If a Panchayat is dissolved, elections are to be
held within:
(a) 1 month
(b) 3 months
(c) 6 months
(d) 1 year
The correct option is (c).
• Article 243E(3) of the Constitution of India
clearly mandates that elections to constitute
a Panchayat must be completed before the
expiry of its duration of five years.
• If a Panchayat is dissolved earlier, elections
must be held within six months from the date
of dissolution.
• This ensures continuity of democratic
governance at the grassroots level and
prevents indefinite administration by
bureaucrats or special officers.
• The State Election Commission (SEC),
constituted under Article 243K, is responsible
for conducting elections to Panchayats.
• However, if the remaining period for the
dissolved Panchayat is less than six months,
elections need not be held (as per proviso to
Article 243E(3)).
• This provision strengthens local selfgovernment under the 73rd Constitutional
Amendment Act, 1992, which gave
constitutional status to Panchayats.

•

The 73rd Amendment added Part IX (Articles
243–243O) to the Constitution.
• Regular elections and fixed terms are a key
element of democratic decentralization,
ensuring accountability and people's
participation.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2) Consider the following statements:
1. The Governor of Punjab is concurrently the
Administrator of Chandigarh
2. The Governor of Kerala is concurrently the
Administrator of Lakshadweep
Which of the above statements is/ are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (a).
• Chandigarh is a Union Territory (UT) and also
the capital of both Punjab and Haryana.
• Since 1984, by a Presidential notification
under Article 239, the Governor of Punjab
has been concurrently appointed as the
Administrator of Chandigarh.
• This arrangement was made for
administrative convenience, as Chandigarh
serves as the shared capital of Punjab and
Haryana.
• Lakshadweep, on the other hand, is
administered by a separate Administrator
appointed by the President under Article 239
of the Constitution.
• The Governor of Kerala does not serve as the
Administrator of Lakshadweep.
• Article 239 states that every Union Territory
shall be administered by the President
through an Administrator appointed by him.
• In some UTs like Delhi and Puducherry, there
are special constitutional provisions (Articles
239AA and 239A respectively), but
Chandigarh follows direct administration.
• This dual role of the Punjab Governor is
unique among Union Territories and reflects
historical and political arrangements after

the Punjab Reorganisation Act, 1966, when
Chandigarh became a UT and was not given to
either state.
• Lakshadweep’s administration is fully under
the control of the Union Government, and its
Administrator is a senior civil servant.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3) In India, the first Municipal Corporation was set
up in which one among the following?
(a) Calcutta
(b) Madras
(c) Bombay
(d) Delhi
The correct option is (b).
• The first Municipal Corporation in India was
established at Madras (now Chennai) in 1687
during British rule.
• This was followed by the establishment of
municipal corporations in Calcutta and
Bombay in 1726.
• These institutions were set up by the British
East India Company primarily to manage
urban affairs, sanitation, taxation, and civic
administration in growing colonial towns.
• Over time, these early corporations evolved
into modern local self-governing institutions.
• The Madras Corporation Act of 1688 laid the
foundation for urban local bodies in India.
• These municipal bodies became important
instruments of colonial governance, enabling
the British to collect taxes and administer
urban services.
• Later, Lord Ripon’s Resolution on Local SelfGovernment (1882) gave a further boost by
introducing the principle of popular
representation in municipal bodies, earning
him the title “Father of Local SelfGovernment in India.”
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4) Consider the following statements:
1. The Advocate General of a State in India is
appointed by the President of India upon the

recommendation of the Governor of the
concerned State.
2. As provided in Civil Procedure Code, High
Courts have original, appellate and advisory
jurisdiction at the State level.
Which of the above statements is/ are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is incorrect:
o The Advocate General of a State is
appointed by the Governor of the
State under Article 165 of the
Constitution, not by the President.
o The person must be qualified to be
appointed as a Judge of a High Court.
o The Advocate General holds office
during the pleasure of the Governor
and acts as the chief legal advisor to
the State Government.
• Statement 2 is incorrect:
o High Courts have original, appellate,
writ, and supervisory jurisdictions.
o Unlike the Supreme Court, High
Courts do not have advisory
jurisdiction; advisory powers are
vested only in the Supreme Court
under Article 143.
o The Civil Procedure Code (CPC)
governs civil procedures but does not
confer advisory functions on High
Courts.
o High Courts entertain original
jurisdiction mainly in civil and
constitutional matters, appellate
jurisdiction over subordinate courts,
and writ jurisdiction under Article
226.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q5) With reference to Lok Adalats, consider the
following statements:
1. An award made by a Lok Adalat is deemed to
be a decree of a civil court and no appeal lies
against thereto before any court.
2. Matrimonial/Family disputes are not covered
under Lok Adalat.
Which of the statements given above is/ are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct:
o Section 21 of the Legal Services
Authorities Act, 1987 provides that
the award of the Lok Adalat shall be
deemed to be a decree of a civil
court.
o It is final and binding on all parties,
and no appeal shall lie to any court
against the award.
o This is aimed at providing speedy,
inexpensive justice and reducing the
burden on regular courts.
• Statement 2 is incorrect:
o Matrimonial and family disputes can
indeed be settled in Lok Adalats
through conciliation and
compromise, especially matters
related to divorce by mutual consent,
maintenance, custody, etc.
o Lok Adalats are also used for motor
accident claims, bank recoveries,
labour disputes, compoundable
criminal offences, etc.
• Lok Adalats are an integral part of Article
39A, which mandates free legal aid and equal
justice.
• The first Lok Adalat was held in 1982 in
Gujarat, and later institutionalized through
the 1987 Act.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q6) With reference to Union Government, consider
the following statements:
1. The Constitution of India provides that all
Cabinet Ministers shall be compulsorily the
sitting members of Lok Sabha only.
2. The Union Cabinet Secretariat operates under
the direction of the Ministry of Parliamentary
Affairs.
Which of the statements given above is/ are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is incorrect:
o Under Article 75(5), a Minister who is
not a member of either House of
Parliament (Lok Sabha or Rajya
Sabha) can hold office for up to six
months, within which he/she must be
elected or nominated to either
House.
o Also, Ministers can be from either
House, not just the Lok Sabha.
• Statement 2 is incorrect:
o The Cabinet Secretariat functions
directly under the Prime Minister,
not under the Ministry of
Parliamentary Affairs.
o It provides secretarial assistance to
the Cabinet, facilitates interministerial coordination, and ensures
implementation of Cabinet decisions.
o The Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs
handles Government’s business in
Parliament, not Cabinet
administration.
• The Cabinet Secretariat plays a central
coordinating role, ensuring smooth
functioning of executive decision-making.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q7) Which one of the following Constitutional
Amendments states that the total number of
Ministers, including the Prime Minister, in the
Council of Ministers shall not exceed fifteen percent
of the total number of members of the House of the
People?
(a) 90th
(b) 91st
(c) 92nd
(d) 93rd
The correct option is (b).
• The 91st Constitutional Amendment Act,
2003 inserted Article 75(1A) at the Union
level and Article 164(1A) at the State level.
• It limits the size of the Council of Ministers to
15% of the total strength of the Lok Sabha at
the Centre and the respective Legislative
Assemblies in the States.
• This amendment aimed to prevent the
formation of jumbo cabinets, which were
often created to accommodate political allies
in coalition governments.
• At the State level, a minimum of 12 Ministers
is required even if 15% works out to a lesser
number.
• This reform sought to promote administrative
efficiency, reduce expenditure, and curb
political patronage.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q8) Consider the following statements:
1. Central Administrative Tribunal (CAT) was set
up during the Prime Ministership of Lal
Bahadur Shastri.
2. The Members of CAT are drawn from both
judicial and administrative streams.
Which of the statements given above is/ are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect:
o The Central Administrative Tribunal
(CAT) was established under the

Administrative Tribunals Act, 1985,
during the Prime Ministership of
Rajiv Gandhi, not Lal Bahadur Shastri.
o It derives authority from Article 323A,
which was inserted by the 42nd
Amendment Act, 1976.
• Statement 2 is correct:
o CAT consists of Judicial Members
(often retired High Court judges) and
Administrative Members (senior civil
servants).
o This hybrid composition ensures both
legal expertise and administrative
experience in deciding service
matters.
• CAT deals exclusively with service matters of
central government employees, providing
speedy and specialized justice, thereby
reducing the burden on regular courts.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q9) With reference to Union Government consider
the following statements:
1. The Ministries and Departments of the
Government of India are created by the Prime
Minister on the advice of the Cabinet
Secretary.
2. Each of the Ministries is assigned to a Minister
by the President of India on the advice of the
Prime Minister.
Which of the statements given above is/ are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect:
o The President of India creates
Ministries and Departments under
Article 77 through the Allocation of
Business Rules, 1961.
o The Prime Minister does not
unilaterally create ministries; this is
done through formal Presidential
action.

o

However, in practice, the President
acts on the aid and advice of the
Council of Ministers, headed by the
PM.
• Statement 2 is correct:
o The President assigns each Ministry
to a Minister on the advice of the
Prime Minister.
o This includes appointment of Cabinet
Ministers, Ministers of State
(Independent charge), and Ministers
of State.
o This reflects the parliamentary
system where the Prime Minister is
the real executive authority, and the
President acts constitutionally on
advice.
• The Allocation of Business Rules and
Transaction of Business Rules govern the
distribution of work among ministries,
ensuring clarity and administrative efficiency.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 2010
Q1) With reference to the Constitution of India,
consider the following:
1. Fundamental Rights
2. Fundamental Duties
3. Directive Principles of State Policy
Which of the above provision of the Constitution of
India is/ are fulfilled by National Social Assistance
Programme launched by Government of India?
(a) 1 only

(b) 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (b).
• The National Social Assistance Programme
(NSAP) is a welfare scheme launched by the
Government of India on 15 August 1995.
• It is implemented by the Ministry of Rural
Development and covers both rural and
urban areas.
• NSAP aims to operationalize the Directive
Principles of State Policy (DPSP), especially
Article 41, which directs the State to provide
public assistance in cases of old age, sickness,
disablement and unemployment.
• NSAP consists of schemes like:
o NOAPS – National Old Age Pension
Scheme
o NFBS – National Family Benefit
Scheme
o NMBS – National Maternity Benefit
Scheme
• NSAP is not a Fundamental Right or
Fundamental Duty programme; it is a DPSPbased welfare initiative aimed at achieving
social security and social justice.
• DPSPs, contained in Part IV (Articles 36–51),
are non-justiciable, but they guide state
policy.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2) Consider the following statements: The Supreme
Court of India tenders advice to the President of
India on matters of law or fact
1. on its own initiative (on any matter of larger
public interest).
2. if he seeks such an advice.
3. only if the matters relate to the Fundamental
Rights of the citizens.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 3 only
(d) 1 and 2
The correct option is (b).

•

Article 143 of the Constitution empowers the
President of India to refer questions of law or
fact of public importance to the Supreme
Court for its advisory opinion.
• Statement 1 is incorrect: The Court cannot
advise on its own; it can give its opinion only
when the President makes a reference.
• Statement 2 is correct: The advisory
jurisdiction is exercised only on a Presidential
reference.
• Statement 3 is incorrect: The advisory opinion
is not restricted to Fundamental Rights; it can
be on any matter of law or fact that the
President considers of public importance.
• The advice given by the Supreme Court is not
binding on the President, though it carries
great persuasive value.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q3) With reference to Lok Adalats, which of the
following statements is correct?
(a) Lok Adalats have the jurisdiction to settle the
matters at pre-litigative stage and not those matters
pending before any court
(b) Lok Adalats can deal with matters which are civil
and not criminal in nature
(c) Every Lok Adalat consists of either serving or
retired judicial officers only and not any other person
(d) None of the statements given above is correct
The correct option is (d).
• Lok Adalats have the jurisdiction to settle
cases both at the pre-litigation stage and
cases pending before any court.
• They can deal with civil matters and
compoundable criminal cases, but not noncompoundable offences.
• Every Lok Adalat bench comprises judicial
officers as well as social workers, lawyers, or
other persons of repute, not just judges.
• Lok Adalats are governed by the Legal
Services Authorities Act, 1987, and their
awards are deemed to be decrees of civil
courts.

•

There is no appeal against a Lok Adalat award,
making them an effective alternative dispute
resolution mechanism.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4) The “Instrument of Instructions” contained in
the Government of India Act 1935 have been
incorporated in the Constitution of India in the year
1950 as
(a) Fundamental Rights
(b) Directive Principles of State Policy
(c) Extent of executive power of State
(d) Conduct of business of the Government of India
The correct option is (b).
• The Instrument of Instructions in the
Government of India Act, 1935 were
guidelines issued to the Governors and the
Executive, meant to direct their policies and
actions.
• These were non-justiciable directions, similar
in spirit to the Directive Principles of State
Policy (DPSP).
• The DPSPs (Part IV, Articles 36–51) of the
Indian Constitution were inspired by these
Instruments of Instructions, as well as from
the Irish Constitution.
• Dr. B.R. Ambedkar described DPSPs as
“instructions to both the legislature and the
executive”.
• They aim to establish social and economic
democracy and are fundamental in
governance of the country.
• Unlike Fundamental Rights, DPSPs are nonenforceable in courts, but the State must
apply them in making laws.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5) Which one of the following is responsible for the
preparation and presentation of Union Budget to the
Parliament?
(a) Department of Revenue
(b) Department of Economic Affairs
(c) Department of Financial Services
(d) Department of Expenditure
The correct option is (b).

•

The Budget Division of the Department of
Economic Affairs (DEA) under the Ministry of
Finance is primarily responsible for preparing
the Union Budget.
• Article 112 of the Constitution mandates the
presentation of the Annual Financial
Statement (Union Budget) before Parliament.
• The DEA issues the Budget Circular,
consolidates receipts and expenditure
estimates from various ministries, and
prepares the Budget documents.
• The Finance Minister presents the Budget in
Parliament on behalf of the Government.
• Other departments of the Ministry of Finance
have distinct functions:
o Department of Revenue → Tax
administration and policy
o Department of Expenditure →
Release of funds and financial control
o Department of Financial Services →
Banking, insurance, pensions
• The Budget reflects government priorities,
allocations, and fiscal policy for the coming
year.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6) With reference to the Consumer Disputes
Redressal at district level in India, which one of the
following statements is not correct?
(a) A State Government can establish more than one
District Forum in a district if it deems fit
(b) One of the members of the District Forum shall be
a woman
(c) The District Forum entertains the complaints
where the value of goods or services does not exceed
rupees fifty lakhs
(d) A complaint … may be filed with a District Forum
by the State Government as a representative of the
interests of the consumers in general
The correct option is (c).
• Under the Consumer Protection Act, 1986
(now replaced by the Consumer Protection
Act, 2019), the pecuniary jurisdiction of
District Forums was up to ₹20 lakh, not ₹50
lakh.

•

The State Government could set up more
than one District Forum in a district
depending on workload.
• One of the members must be a woman,
ensuring gender representation.
• Complaints can be filed by the State or
Central Government in the interest of
consumers at large.
• The 2019 Act has since enhanced jurisdiction
and renamed District Forums as District
Commissions.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7) Who of the following shall cause every
recommendation made by the Finance Commission
to be laid before each House of Parliament?
(a) The President of India
(b) The Speaker of Lok Sabha
(c) The Prime Minister of India
(d) The Union Finance Minister
The correct option is (a).
• Article 280 provides for the constitution of a
Finance Commission by the President every
five years or earlier if needed.
• Article 281 specifically states that the
President shall cause every recommendation
made by the Finance Commission, together
with an explanatory memorandum as to the
action taken, to be laid before each House of
Parliament.
• This ensures transparency and parliamentary
scrutiny of the recommendations regarding
tax devolution, grants-in-aid, and financial
relations between the Centre and the States.
• The Finance Commission plays a crucial role in
ensuring fiscal federalism.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q8) Which one of the following authorities makes
recommendation to the Governor of a State as to the
principles for determining the taxes and duties which
may be appropriated by the Panchayats in that
particular State?
(a) District Planning Committees
(b) State Finance Commission

(c) Finance Ministry of that State
(d) Panchayati Raj Ministry of that State
The correct option is (b).
• Article 243-I mandates the Governor of a
State to constitute a State Finance
Commission (SFC) every five years.
• The SFC reviews:
o The financial position of Panchayats
and Municipalities
o Recommends principles for
distribution of taxes, duties, tolls,
and fees between the State and
Panchayats
o Suggests measures to improve
financial autonomy of local bodies
• The Governor must lay the SFC’s report
before the State Legislature, along with an
explanatory memorandum on the action
taken.
• District Planning Committees (Article 243ZD)
focus on integrating plans of Panchayats and
Municipalities—not on taxation or financial
devolution.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2011
Q1) Consider the following statements: In India, a
metropolitan planning committee:
1. Is constituted under the provisions of the
Constitution of India.
2. Prepares the draft development plans for
metropolitan area.
3. Has the sole responsibility for implementing
government sponsored schemes in the
metropolitan area.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (a).
• Metropolitan Planning Committees (MPCs)
are provided for under Article 243ZE of the
Constitution, inserted by the 74th

Constitutional Amendment Act, 1992 (Part
IX-A dealing with municipalities).
• MPCs are constituted in every metropolitan
area, which means an area with a population
of 10 lakh or more.
• MPCs prepare draft development plans for
the metropolitan area as a whole, integrating
the plans prepared by municipalities and
panchayats.
• They must take into account plans prepared
by local bodies, matters of common interest,
and consult both municipalities and
panchayats, while also considering State and
Central priorities.
• MPCs do not have the sole responsibility for
implementing government schemes;
implementation is carried out by multiple
agencies, including local bodies and state
departments.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q2) What is the difference between “vote-onaccount” and “interim budget”?
1. The provision of a “vote-on-account’’ is used
by a regular government, while an “interim
budget’’ is a provision used by a caretaker
government.
2. A “vote-on-account’’ only deals with the
expenditure in government budget, while an
“interim budget’’ includes both expenditure
and receipts.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (b).
• A Vote-on-Account is a constitutional
provision under Article 116, authorizing the
government to withdraw money from the
Consolidated Fund of India for a short period
(generally 2–4 months) to meet essential
expenditure until the Budget is passed.
• It only deals with the expenditure side and
does not include receipts.

•

An Interim Budget is a complete set of
accounts, including both receipts and
expenditure, presented when a regular
budget cannot be passed (e.g., in an election
year).
• Any government—regular or caretaker—can
present either; there is no constitutional
restriction linking the two to caretaker or
regular governments.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q3) The Constitution (Seventy-third Amendment)
Act, 1992 … provides for which of the following?
1. Constitution of District Planning Committees
2. State Election Commissions to conduct all
Panchayat elections
3. Establishment of State Finance Commission
State the correct answer using the codes given below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• The 73rd Constitutional Amendment Act,
1992 added Part IX to the Constitution,
dealing with Panchayati Raj.
• It provides for:
o State Election Commissions (Article
243K) to conduct all Panchayat
elections.
o State Finance Commissions (Article
243I) to review Panchayat finances
and recommend revenue-sharing
every 5 years.
• The District Planning Committees (DPCs) are
provided for by Article 243ZD, which belongs
to the 74th Amendment Act, related to urban
local bodies.

•

The Eleventh Schedule (added by 73rd
Amendment) lists 29 functional items for
Panchayats.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4) Consider the following:
1. Right to education
2. Right to equal access to public service
3. Right to food
Which of the above is/are Human Right/Human Rights
under “Universal Declaration of Human Rights”?
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2 only
(c) 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• The Universal Declaration of Human Rights
(UDHR), adopted by the UN General Assembly
in 1948, recognizes several civil, political,
economic, social, and cultural rights.
• Right to Education → Article 26 of UDHR.
• Equal access to public service → Article 21(2)
of UDHR.
• Right to adequate standard of living,
including food, clothing, housing, medical care
→ Article 25 of UDHR.
• All three rights are thus included as human
rights under the UDHR.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q5) In India, if a religious sect/community is given
the status of a national minority, what special
advantages is it entitled to?
1. It can establish and administer exclusive
educational institutions.
2. The President of India automatically
nominates a representative of the community
to Lok Sabha.
3. It can derive benefits from the Prime
Minister’s 15-Point Programme.
Which of the statements give above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3

The correct option is (c).
• Article 30 of the Constitution grants
minorities (religious or linguistic) the right to
establish and administer educational
institutions of their choice.
• There is no automatic provision for
nomination to the Lok Sabha for religious
minorities. The Anglo-Indian community had
special nomination under Article 331 (now
abolished), but this did not apply to all
minorities.
• Minorities notified under Section 2(c) of the
National Commission for Minorities Act,
1992 are eligible for benefits under the Prime
Minister’s 15-Point Programme, which
includes schemes for education, credit, skill
development, and employment.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6) India is home to lakhs of persons with
disabilities… what benefits are available under law?
1. Free schooling till the age of 18 years in
government-run schools
2. Preferential allotment of land for setting up
business
3. Ramps in public buildings
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Under the Persons with Disabilities (Equal
Opportunities, Protection of Rights and Full
Participation) Act, 1995, then in force:
o Section 26: Provides for free
education for children with
disabilities up to the age of 18 years.
o Governments provided preferential
allotment of land for housing,
business, and other purposes to
disabled persons.
o Public buildings were required to be
made barrier-free, including ramps
and other accessibility measures.

•

These provisions were further expanded
under the RPwD Act, 2016, which provides
for broader rights and accessibility standards.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q7) The authorization for the withdrawal of funds
from the Consolidated Fund of India must come from
(a) The President of India
(b) The Parliament of India
(c) The Prime Minister of India
(d) The Union Finance Minister
The correct option is (b).
• Article 266(3) of the Constitution: “No money
shall be withdrawn from the Consolidated
Fund of India except under appropriation
made by law.”
• This law is passed by Parliament through the
Appropriation Act.
• The President only assents to the Bill; he/she
cannot authorize withdrawal without
parliamentary sanction.
• Even Vote-on-Account and Supplementary
Grants require approval by Parliament.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q8) All revenues received by the Union Government
by way of taxes and other receipts for the conduct of
Government business are credited to the
(a) Contingency Fund of India
(b) Public Account
(c) Consolidated Fund of India
(d) Deposits and Advances Fund
The correct option is (c).
• Article 266(1): All revenues received by the
Union Government (tax and non-tax), all loans
raised by it, and all money received in
repayment of loans are credited to the
Consolidated Fund of India (CFI).
• Public Account of India (Article 266(2)) deals
with trust money like provident funds, small
savings, etc., and does not require
parliamentary appropriation.
• Contingency Fund (Article 267) is for urgent,
unforeseen expenditure and is operated by
the President.

•

Withdrawals from CFI require parliamentary
approval.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q9) When the annual budget is not passed by the
Lok Sabha
(a) The budget is modified and presented again
(b) The budget is referred to the Rajya Sabha for
suggestions
(c) The Union Finance Minister is asked to resign
(d) The Prime Minister submits the resignation of
Council of Ministers
The correct option is (d).
• The Budget is presented as a Money Bill
(Article 110).
• If the Lok Sabha rejects the Budget, it
amounts to a no-confidence motion against
the Council of Ministers.
• The government must resign in such a
situation.
• The Rajya Sabha has no power to reject
Money Bills (Article 109).
• Modifying and re-presenting is not a
constitutional remedy for defeat of the
Budget.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q10) Under the Constitution of India, which one of
the following is not a Fundamental Duty?
(a) To vote in public elections
(b) To develop the scientific temper
(c) To safeguard public property
(d) To abide the Constitution and respect its ideals
The correct option is (a).
• Article 51A lists the Fundamental Duties of
citizens, added by the 42nd Constitutional
Amendment, 1976, and expanded by the 86th
Amendment (2002).
• These include:
o To abide by the Constitution
o To develop scientific temper
o To safeguard public property
o To protect environment, etc.

•

Voting is a statutory right under the
Representation of the People Act, not a
Fundamental Duty.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q11) With reference to the Finance Commission of
India, which of the following statements is correct?
(a) It encourages the inflow of foreign capital for
infrastructure development
(b) It facilitates the proper distribution of finances
among the public sector undertakings
(c) It ensures transparency in financial administration
(d) None of the statements (a), (b) and (c) given above
is correct in this context
The correct option is (d).
• The Finance Commission is constituted by the
President under Article 280 every 5 years.
• Its functions are to recommend the
distribution of taxes between the Union and
States, and among the States themselves
(vertical and horizontal devolution), and to
recommend grants-in-aid.
• It does not deal with foreign capital, PSU
finances, or general transparency
mechanisms.
• Its recommendations are laid before
Parliament along with an explanatory
memorandum (Article 281).
Hence, option (d) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 2012
Q1) In India other than ensuring that public funds are
used efficiently and for intended purpose what is the
importance of the office of the CAG?
1. CAG exercises exchequer control on behalf of
the Parliament when the President of India
declares national emergency/ financial
emergency
2. CAG reports on the execution of projects or
programmes by the ministries are discussed
by the Public Accounts Committee
3. Information from CAG reports can be used by
investigating agencies to press charges against

those who have violated the law while
managing public finances.
4. While dealing with audit and accounting of
govt. companies. CAG has certain judicial
powers for prosecuting those who violate the
law.
Which of the above are correct?
(a) 1, 3 and 4 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (c)
• (1) Exchequer control during Emergencies —
NO.
Arts. 148–151 define the CAG as independent
auditor of Union/States; it does not exercise
“control” over spending even during National
Emergency (Art. 352) or Financial Emergency
(Art. 360). Control remains via constitutional
finance procedures and Parliament.
• (2) PAC discussion — YES.
CAG’s reports (Appropriation, Finance,
Compliance/Performance Audits, PSU reports)
are routinely examined by the PAC; CAG is
traditionally its “friend, philosopher, and
guide.”
• (3) Use by investigating agencies — YES.
CAG findings are credible evidence inputs;
police/CVC/ED/CBI, etc., may initiate probes
based on audit flags. CAG does not prosecute,
but enables others to do so.
• (4) Judicial powers — NO.
CAG has no adjudicatory/prosecutorial
powers. It audits, certifies, and reports.
Hence, (c) is correct.
Q2) The Prime Minister of India, at the time of his/
her appointment
(a) need not necessarily be a member of one of the
Houses of the Parliament but must become a member
of one of the Houses within six months
(b) need not necessarily be a member of one of the
Houses of the Parliament but must become a member
of the Lok Sabha within six months
(c) must be a member of one of the Houses of the

parliament
(d) must be a member of the Lok Sabha
Answer: (a)
• Art. 75: President appoints PM;
conventionally the Lok Sabha leader;
membership flexibility exists.
• SC (1997) & Art. 75(5) principle: non-member
can be minister/PM for 6 months, must enter
either House within that period.
• Past PMs (e.g., Manmohan Singh) served
from Rajya Sabha, proving LS membership not
mandatory.
• Only “one of the Houses” is required within
six months, not necessarily LS.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3) With reference to the Delimitation Commission,
consider the following statements:
1. The orders of the Delimitation Commission
cannot be challenged in a Court of Law.
2. When the orders of the Delimitation
Commission are laid before the Lok Sabha or
State Legislative Assembly, they cannot effect
any modifications in the orders.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (c)
• Set up under Delimitation Acts
(1952/62/72/2002); a high-power body.
• Orders have force of law and are final, not
justiciable in courts.
• Houses only lay the orders; no modifications
permissible.
• Orders come into force on date notified by
the President.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4) According to the Constitution of India, it is the
duty of the President of India to cause to be laid
before the Parliament which of the following?
1. The Recommendations of the Union Finance
Commission

2. The Report of the Public Accounts Committee
3. The Report of the Comptroller and Auditor
General
4. The Report of the National Commission for
the Scheduled Castes
Select the correct answer the using the codes given
below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 4 only
(c) 1, 3 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (c)
• Art. 281: Finance Commission
recommendations to be laid by President
with action taken memo.
• Art. 151: CAG reports are submitted to
President, who lays them before Parliament.
• Art. 338: NCSC reports presented to

with action taken notes.
• PAC reports are Parliamentary committee

Q5) A deadlock between the Lok Sabha and Rajya
Sabha calls for a joint sitting of the parliament during
the passage of
1. Ordinary legislation
2. Money bill
3. Constitution Amendment bill
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1,2 and 3
Answer: (a)
• Art. 108: Joint sitting possible only for
ordinary bills under specified deadlock
conditions.
• Money Bills: RS cannot block; no joint sitting
(Art. 109).
• Constitution Amendment Bills: must be
separately passed by each House; no joint
sitting (Art. 368).

•

Speaker presides at joint sitting.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q6) How do District Rural Development Agencies
(DRDAs) help in the reduction of rural poverty in
India?
1. DRDAs act as Panchayati Raj Institutions in
certain specified backward regions of the
country.
2. DRDAs undertake area-specific scientific study
of the cause of poverty and malnutrition and
prepare detailed remedial measures.
3. DRDAs secure inter-sectoral and interdepartmental coordination and cooperation
for effective implementation of anti- poverty
programmes.
4. DRDAs watch over and ensure effective
utilisation of the funds intended for antipoverty programme.
Which of the statements given above is/ are correct?
(a) 1, 2 and 3 only
(b) 3 and 4 only
(c) 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (b)
• DRDA is a registered society, not a
Panchayati Raj Institution; it supports PRIs,
doesn’t replace them.
• Core roles: coordination across
departments/sectors for anti-poverty
schemes (#3 true).
• Fund flow/usage oversight and monitoring
for programmes like SGSY/NRLM, IAY/PMAYG, MGNREGS (support) (#4 true).
• While diagnostics may occur, formal
“scientific studies” remit isn’t a defined
statutory function (#2 not core/standard).
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q8) What is the provision to safeguard the autonomy
of the Supreme Court of India?
1. While appointing the Supreme Court judges,
the President of India has to consult the Chief
Justice of India.
2. The SC judges can be removed by the Chief
Justice of India only
3. The salaries of judges are charged on the
Consolidated Fund of India to which the
legislature does not have to vote.
4. All appointments of officers and staffs of the
SC are made by the Government only after
consulting the Chief Justice of India
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 3 only
(b) 3 and 4 only
(c) 4 only
(d) 1,2,3 and 4
Answer: (a)
• Art. 124: President consults CJI (and others) in
judge appointments; collegium evolved by SC
rulings.

President’s order, not by CJI (#2 false).

•
•

Judges’ salaries are charged on CFI; nonvotable head (#3 true).

not “by Government after consulting CJI” (#4

Q9) In the areas covered under the Panchayat

2. Gram Sabha has the ownership of minor

•

state/centre; “recommendation required”

Q10) In the Parliament of India, the purpose of an
adjournment motion is
(a) to allow a discussion on a definite matter of urgent
public importance
(b) to let opposition members collect information
from the ministers
(c) to allow a reduction of specific amount in demand
for grant
(d) to postpone the proceedings to check the
inappropriate or violent behaviour on the part of
some members
Answer: (a)
• Adjournment Motion: for urgent, definite
public importance, interrupting normal
business.
• Carries an element of censure; admitted at
Speaker’s discretion.
• Cut Motions (not adjournment) reduce
demands for grants.
• Adjournment/Suspension for disorder is
Chair’s procedural power, not this motion’s
purpose.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q11) The National Green Tribunal Act, 2010 was
enacted in consonance with which of the following
provisions of the Constitution of India?
1. Right to healthy environment, construed as a
part of Right to life under Article 21
2. Provision of grants for raising the level of
administration in the Scheduled Areas for the
welfare of Scheduled Tribes under Article
275(1)
3. Powers and functions of Gram Sabha as
mentioned under Article 243 (A)
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1 only

(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1,2 and 3
Answer: (a)
• NGT Act, 2010 aims at effective
environmental adjudication, aligned with Art.
21 expanded to right to clean environment.
• Also echoes Art. 48A & 51A(g) (DPSP/Duty),
but option focuses on Art. 21.
• Art. 275(1) (grants to Scheduled Areas) and
Art. 243A (Gram Sabha functions) are not the
basis of NGT Act.
• NGT has original/appellate jurisdiction over
environmental statutes.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q12) Consider the following provisions under the
Directive Principles of State Policy as enshrined in
the Constitution of India:
1. Securing for citizens of India a uniform civil
code
2. Organising village Panchayats
3. Promoting cottage industries in rural areas
4. Securing for all the workers reasonable leisure
and cultural opportunities.
Which of the above are the Gandhian Principles that
are reflected in the Directive Principles of State
Policy?
(a) 1, 2 and 4 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1, 3 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (d)
• Gandhian DPSPs include village panchayats
(Art. 40), cottage industries (Art. 43), UCC
(Art. 44 often grouped under liberalintellectual but frequently listed with
Gandhian aims in many prep sources), and
leisure/cultural opportunities (Art. 43).
• Emphasis on rural self-government, selfreliance, community life, worker welfare.
• UPSC key lists all four under Gandhianoriented aims for that year.

•

Reflect the social reconstruction vision
aligned to Gandhian thought.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q13) Consider the following statements:
1. Union territories are not represented in the
Rajya Sabha
2. It is within the purview of the Chief Election
Commissioner to adjudicate the election
disputes
3. According to the constitution of India,
Parliament consists of Lok Sabha and Rajya
Sabha only
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 Only
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1and 3 only
(d) None
Answer: (d)
• UTs like Delhi & Puducherry have RS seats
(Fourth Schedule) → #1 false.
• Election disputes are tried by High Courts
(appellate to SC); CEC/ECI conducts elections,
doesn’t adjudicate petitions → #2 false.
• Parliament = President + RS + LS (Art. 79), not
just the two Houses → #3 false.
• None of the statements is correct.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q14) With reference to consumer’s rights / privileges
under the provision of law in India which of the
following statements correct?
1. Consumer are empowered to take samples for
food testing
2. When consumer files a complaint in any
consumer forum, no fee is required to be
paid.
3. In case of death of consumer, his/her legal
heir can file a complaint in the consumer
forum on his/her behalf.
Select the correct answer using the codes given below
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1,2 and 3

Answer: (c)
• Under food safety/weights laws,
consumers/inspectors may seek
sampling/testing pathways (#1 broadly
acceptable in exam context).
• CPA, 1986 (then): court fee payable per claim
slab; not “no fee” → #2 false.
• Legal representatives/heirs can
continue/initiate complaints on consumer’s
death → #3 true.
• Forums: District/State/National Commissions
with pecuniary limits (then).
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q15) Regarding the office of the Lok Sabha speaker
consider the following statements:
1. He/She holds the office during the pleasure of
the President
2. He/She need not be a member of the house at
the time of his/her election but has to
become a member of the House within six
months from the date of his/her election
3. If he/she intends to resign the letter of his/her
resignation has to be addressed to the Deputy
Speaker.
Which of the above statements given above is/are
correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3 only
(c) 1,2 and 3
(d) None
Answer: (b)
• Speaker does not hold office at President’s
pleasure; removable by LS resolution (Art. 94)
→ #1 false.
• Must be an LS member to be elected
Speaker; no six-month window → #2 false.
• Resignation is addressed to the Deputy
Speaker → #3 true.
• Continues till immediately before first
meeting of next LS unless removed.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q16) Which of the following are included in the
original jurisdiction of the Supreme Court?
1. A dispute between the Government of India
and one or more States
2. A dispute regarding elections to either House
of the parliament or that of Legislature of a
State
3. A dispute between the Government of India
and Union Territory
4. A dispute between two or more States.
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 4
(d) 3 and 4
Answer: (c)
• Art. 131: SC original jurisdiction over Centre–
State and Inter-State disputes (#1 & #4 true).
• Election disputes go to HCs/SC under
Representation of the People Act (not under
Art. 131) → #2 false.
• UTs are not “States” for Art. 131 Centre–UT
disputes context → #3 not covered.
• Original jurisdiction is exclusive in these
federal disputes.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q17) Which of the following special powers have
been conferred on the Rajya Sabha by the
Constitution of India?
(a) To change the existing territory of a State and to
change the name of a State
(b) To pass a resolution empowering the Parliament to
make laws in the State List and to create one or more
All India Services
(c) To amend the election procedure of the President
and to determine the pension of the President after
his/her retirement
(d) To determine the functions of the Election
Commission and to determine the number of Election
Commissioners.
Answer: (b)

•

•
•
•

Art. 249: RS can empower Parliament to
legislate on State List in national interest
(2/3rd present & voting).
Art. 312: RS can create All-India Services by
2/3rd resolution.
Changing state names/areas is via Parliament
(Art. 3), not a special RS power.
EC functions/strength via Parliamentary law
generally; no RS-exclusive provision.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q18) Which of the following are the methods of
Parliamentary control over public finance in India?
1. Placing Annual Financial Statement before the
Parliament
2. Withdrawal of moneys from Consolidated
Fund of India only after passing the
Appropriation Bill
3. Provisions of supplementary grants and voteon account
4. A periodic or at least a mid-year review of
programme of the Government against
macroeconomic forecasts and expenditure by
a Parliamentary Budget Office.
5. Introducing Finance Bill in the Parliament
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1, 2, 3 and 5 only
(b) 1, 2 and 4 only
(c) 3, 4 and 5 only
(d) 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5
Answer: (d)
• Art. 112: Annual Financial Statement
(Budget) laid before Parliament.
• Art. 114/266: Appropriation Act authorizes
CFI withdrawals.
• Supplementary/Excess grants & VoA are
formal control tools.
• Legislative scrutiny can include mid-year
reviews/committee oversight; UPSC treated
PBO-style review as part of parliamentary
control mechanisms.
• Finance Bill (Art. 110/117) for taxation
changes is a key control instrument.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q19) Which of the following provisions of the
Constitution of India have a bearing on Education?
1. Directive Principles of State Policy
2. Rural and Urban Local Bodies
3. Fifth Schedule
4. Sixth Schedule
5. Seventh Schedule
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3, 4 and 5 only
(c) 1, 2 and 5 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 4 and 5
Answer: (c)
• DPSP (Art. 41, 45 → later Art. 21A) guide
education goals/policy.
• 73rd/74th CAA (11th/12th Schedules) assign
education-related functions to PRIs/ULBs.
• Seventh Schedule distributes education
between Union/States (post-42nd:
Concurrent List).
• Fifth/Sixth Schedules relate to
Scheduled/Tribal Areas administration; not
direct constitutional allocation for education
in this question’s framing.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2013
Q1) What will follow if a Money Bill is substantially
amended by the Rajya Sabha?
(a) The Lok Sabha may still proceed with the Bill,
accepting or not accepting the recommendations of
the Rajya Sabha
(b) The Lok Sabha cannot consider the Bill further
(c) The Lok Sabha may send the Bill to the Rajya Sabha
for reconsideration
(d) The President may call a joint sitting for passing
the Bill
Answer: (a)
• Art. 110: Money Bills can be introduced only
in Lok Sabha; Speaker’s certificate is final.
• Rajya Sabha has no power to amend; it can
recommend changes within 14 days.

•
•

Lok Sabha may accept or reject
recommendations; bill deemed passed in
original or amended form.
If RS doesn’t return in 14 days → deemed
passed in LS form.
No joint sitting applies to Money Bills (Art.
108 excluded).
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q2) Which one of the following statements is
correct?
(a) In India, the same person cannot be appointed as
Governor for two or more States at the same time
(b) The Judges of the High Court of the States in India
are appointed by the Governor of the State just as the
Judges of the Supreme Court are appointed by the
President
(c) No procedure has been laid down in the
Constitution of India for the removal of a Governor
from his/her post
(d) In the case of a Union Territory having a legislative
setup, the Chief Minister is appointed by the Lt.
Governor on the basis of majority support
Answer: (c)
• Art. 153–167: Governor = dual role (State
head + Centre’s agent).
• 7th Amendment (1956) allows same person
as Governor for 2+ states.
• HC Judges appointed by President, not
Governor (Art. 217).
• Governor serves at President’s pleasure; no
constitutional removal procedure/grounds
specified.
• In UTs with legislature (e.g., Delhi,
Puducherry), President appoints CM, not LG
directly.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3) Consider the following statements:
1. An amendment to the Constitution of India
can be initiated by an introduction of a bill in
the Lok Sabha only.
2. If such an amendment seeks to make changes
in the federal character of the Constitution,

the amendment also requires to be ratified by
the legislature of all the States of India.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (d)
• Art. 368: Amendment can be introduced in
either House (Lok Sabha or Rajya Sabha).
• Requires special majority in both Houses; no
joint sitting provision.
• Federal amendments need ratification by
half of the States, not all.
• Ratification has no time limit; simple majority
in State legislatures is enough.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4) Consider the following statements: Attorney
General of India can
1. take part in the proceedings of the Lok Sabha
2. be a member of a committee of the Lok Sabha
3. speak in the Lok Sabha
4. vote in the Lok Sabha
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 4
(c) 1, 2 and 3
(d) 1 and 3 only
Answer: (c)
• Art. 76: AGI has right to speak & participate
in Lok Sabha & its committees.
• Not a member of Parliament but enjoys
parliamentary privileges.
• No voting rights in House.
• Can attend committee proceedings, advise
government, speak when permitted.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5) Which of the following bodies does not/do not
find mention in the Constitution?
1. National Development Council
2. Planning Commission
3. Zonal Councils

Select the correct answer using the codes given
below.
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (d)
• NDC (1952) created by executive resolution,
not constitutional/statutory.
• Planning Commission (1950) created by
Cabinet resolution, extra-constitutional.
• Zonal Councils = statutory bodies under
States Reorganisation Act 1956, not
constitutional.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q6) The Parliament can make any law for whole or
any part of India for implementing international
treaties
(a) with the consent of all the States
(b) with the consent of the majority of States
(c) with the consent of the States concerned
(d) without the consent of any State
Answer: (d)
• Art. 253: Parliament can legislate on State
subjects to implement treaties/agreements.
• State consent not required; gives Centre
overriding power to fulfil international
obligations.
• Examples: TRIPS, Geneva Convention Act
(1960), Environment Acts, etc.
• Also one of five exceptional circumstances
Parliament can legislate on State List.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q7) The Government enacted the Panchayat
Extension to Scheduled Areas (PESA) Act in 1996.
Which one of the following is not identified as its
objective?
(a) To provide self-governance
(b) To recognize traditional rights
(c) To create autonomous regions in tribal areas
(d) To free tribal people from exploitation
Answer: (c)

•
•

•
•

PESA extends Part IX to Fifth Schedule Areas
with modifications.
Objectives include self-rule, recognition of
customary practices, Gram Sabha
empowerment, protection from exploitation.
It does not create “autonomous regions”
(that’s Sixth Schedule in NE states).
Emphasises participatory democracy and
tribal self-governance.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q8) Under the Scheduled Tribes and Other
Traditional Forest Dwellers (Recognition of Forest
Rights) Act, 2006, who shall be the authority to
initiate the process for determining the nature and
extent of individual or community forest rights or
both?
(a) State Forest Department
(b) District Collector/Deputy Commissioner
(c) Tahsildar /Block Development Officer / Mandal
Revenue Officer
(d) Gram Sabha
Answer: (d)
• Gram Sabha is the initiating authority under
FRA 2006.
• It receives claims, verifies, and forwards
recommendations to higher committees.
• Section 6 assigns GS a central role in rights
determination.
• Sub-Divisional & District Committees
adjudicate based on GS recommendations.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q9) ‘Economic Justice’ the objectives of Constitution
has been provided in
(a) the Preamble and Fundamental Rights
(b) the Preamble and the Directive Principles of State
Policy
(c) the Fundamental Rights and the Directive
Principles of State Policy
(d) None of the above
Answer: (b)
• Preamble includes Justice — social,
economic, political.

•
•
•

DPSPs (esp. Art. 38, 39) guide economic
justice through state policy.
FRs ensure political/legal justice; economic
aims mainly in DPSPs.
Reflects welfare state and distributive justice
concept.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q10) According to the Constitution of India, which of
the following are fundamental for the governance of
the country?
(a) Fundamental Rights
(b) Fundamental Duties
(c) Directive Principles of State Policy
(d) Fundamental Rights and Fundamental Duties
Answer: (c)
• Art. 37 declares DPSPs are “fundamental to
governance”.
• Guide legislative & administrative policy;
non-justiciable but binding in spirit.
• Aim to establish economic & social
democracy, complementing FRs.
• Called “instrument of instructions” to the
State.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Chair usually from Opposition.

Q12) In the context of India, which of the following
principles is/are implied institutionally in the
parliamentary government?
1. Members of the Cabinet are Members of the
Parliament.
2. Ministers hold the office till they enjoy
confidence in the Parliament.

Select the correct answer using the codes given
below.
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 3 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (a)

Non-MPs must join within 6 months.
• Collective responsibility → Ministers hold
office till LS confidence retained.
• Cabinet is headed by PM, not Head of State
(President).
• Reflects Westminster model features.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 2014

Q1) Which one of the following Schedules of the
Constitution of India contains provisions regarding
anti-defection?
(a) Second Schedule
(b) Fifth Schedule
(c) Eighth Schedule
d) Tenth Schedule
Answer: (d)
• Tenth Schedule (1985): inserted by 52nd
Amendment to curb defection.
• Lays disqualification grounds for switching
parties/whips violation.
• Presiding officer decides (subject to judicial
review after Kihoto Hollohan).
• Strengthened by 91st Amendment (2003)
limiting size of ministries/splits.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q2) In the Constitution of India, promotion of
international peace and security is included in the
(a) Preamble
(b) Directive Principles of State Policy
(c) Fundamental Duties
(d) Ninth Schedule
Answer: (b)

•
•
•
•

Article 51 (DPSP): promote international
peace & security.
Includes just relations, respect for
international law, arbitration.
Non-justiciable but fundamental to
governance (Art. 37).
Guides India’s foreign policy orientation.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q3) Which of the following are associated with
‘Planning’ in India?
1.The Finance Commission
2. The National Development Council
3. The Union Ministry of Rural Development
4. The Union Ministry of Urban Development
5. The Parliament
Select the correct answer using the code given below
(a) 1, 2 and 5 only
(b) 1, 3 and 4 only
(c) 2 and 5 only
(d) 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5
Answer: (c)
• NDC (executive body, 1952): apex forum for
plan policy & approval.
• Parliament: passes Plans via
budget/appropriation, oversight.
• Finance Commission → devolution, not
planning.
• Line ministries (Rural/Urban Dev.) implement,
not apex planning bodies.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4) Which of the following is/are the function(s) of
the Cabinet Secretariat?
1. Preparation of agenda for Cabinet Meetings
2. Secretarial assistance to Cabinet Committees
3. Allocation of financial resources to the
Ministries
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 1 and 2 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (c)

•
•
•
•

Cabinet Secretariat: handles agenda,
minutes, circulation, follow-up.
Provides secretarial support to
Cabinet/Cabinet Committees.
Does not allocate funds; that’s Ministry of
Finance (Budget/Expenditure).
Ensures inter-ministerial coordination under
Transaction/Allocation Rules.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q5) Consider the following statements: A
Constitutional Government is one which
1. places effective restrictions on individual
liberty in the interest of State Authority
2. places effective restrictions on the Authority
of the State in the interest of individual liberty
Select the correct answer using the code given below.
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (b)
• Constitutionalism = limited government;
limits State power.
• Protects individual liberty via checks &
balances/rights/judicial review.
• Statement 1 contradicts liberty-protecting
essence.
• Emphasizes rule of law, non-arbitrariness,
accountability.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6) Which of the following are the discretionary
powers given to the Governor of a State?
1. Sending a report to the President for
President’s Rule
2. Appointing the Ministers
3. Reserving certain bills for President’s
consideration
4. Making rules to conduct State Government
business
Select the correct answer using the code given
below.
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 1 and 3 only

(c) 2, 3 and 4 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (b)
• Discretionary: Art. 356 report to President;
reserve bills (esp. affecting Union powers).
• Appointing ministers → done on CM’s advice
(not discretion).
• Business rules framed by Governor on CM’s
advice (Art. 166(3)).
• Also discretion in hung assembly CM choice,
but not blanket for (2)/(4).
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7) Consider the following statements:
1. The President shall make rules for convenient

among Ministers.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (a)
• Art. 77(3): President makes
Transaction/Allocation of Business Rules.
• Art. 77(1): Executive action expressed in the
name of the President, not PM.
• Authentication rules under Art. 77(2).
• Reflects parliamentary executive under
President’s name.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q8) Consider the following statements regarding a
No Confidence Motion in India:
1. No mention in the Constitution.
2. Can be introduced in the Lok Sabha only.
Which of the statements given above is / are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (c)
• No-confidence not in Constitution; arises
from Art. 75(3) collective responsibility.
• Rule 198, Lok Sabha: procedure; 50 members
support to admit.
• Only Lok Sabha controls Council of Ministers;
RS cannot oust Govt.
• If passed → Council resigns/advises
dissolution.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q9) The power to increase the number of judges in
the Supreme Court of India is vested in
(a) President
(b) Parliament
(c) CJI
(d) Law Commission
Answer: (b)
• Parliament by law regulates SC
composition/jurisdiction (Art. 124).
• Strength revised multiple times by Acts of
Parliament.
• President appoints judges but cannot alter
strength unilaterally.
• Law Commission only recommends.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q10) The power of the Supreme Court to decide
Centre–State disputes falls under its
(a) advisory
(b) appellate
(c) original
(d) writ
Answer: (c)
• Art. 131: Exclusive original jurisdiction in
Centre–State / inter-State disputes.

•

Involves legal right questions (law or fact).
Distinct from Art. 32 writs (FR enforcement) &
appellate powers.
Advisory (Art. 143) = President’s references;
not for such disputes.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q11) Which one of the following is the largest
Committee of the Parliament?
(a) Public Accounts
(b) Estimates
(c) Public Undertakings
(d) Petitions
Answer: (b)
• Estimates Committee: 30 members (LS only)
→ largest.
• PAC: 22 (15 LS + 7 RS); PUC: 22; Petitions (LS):
15.
• Estimates examines budget estimates,
suggests economy & efficiency.
• Aids in financial control & administrative
reforms.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 2015
Q1. The provisions in the Fifth Schedule and Sixth
Schedule in the Constitution of India are made in
order to
(a) protect the interests of Scheduled Tribes
(b) determine the boundaries between states
(c) determine the powers, authorities, and
responsibilities of Panchayats
(d) protect the interests of all the border States
Answer: (a)

•

•

Fifth Schedule: safeguards Scheduled
Areas/Tribes in non-Northeast regions.
Sixth Schedule: deals with Autonomous
District Councils in NE (Assam, Meghalaya,
Tripura, Mizoram).
Aim: protect cultural identity, selfgovernance, prevent exploitation.
President has power to declare Scheduled
Areas.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q2. With reference to the Union Government
consider the following statements.
1. The Department of Revenue is responsible for
the preparation of Union Budget that is
presented to the parliament
2. No amount can be withdrawn from the
Consolidated Fund of India without the
authorization of Parliament of India.
3. All the disbursements made from Public
Account also need the Authorization from the
Parliament of India
Which of the following statements given above is/ are
correct?
(a) 1 and 2 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 2 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (c)
• Budget is prepared by the Department of
Economic Affairs, not Revenue.
• Article 266: Parliamentary authorization
required for withdrawals from Consolidated
Fund of India.
• Public Account is operated by executive
action — no parliamentary vote needed.
• Dept. of Revenue mainly deals with tax
collection.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Who/Which of the following is the Custodian of
the Constitution of India?
(a) The President of India
(b) The Prime Minister of India

(c) The Lok Sabha Secretariat
(d) The Supreme Court of India
Answer: (d)
• Supreme Court ensures that laws and
executive actions are constitutional.
• Exercises judicial review (Articles 13, 32, 131,
136 etc.).
• Interprets and protects the basic structure of
the Constitution.
• Acts as guardian and ultimate interpreter.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q4. Consider the following statements:
1. The Legislative Council of a state in India can
be larger in size than half of the Legislative
Assembly of that particular state.
2. The Governor of a state nominates the
Chairman of Legislative Council of that
particular state.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (d)
• Legislative Council: max size 1/3rd of
Legislative Assembly; cannot exceed half.
• Chairman is elected by the Council itself, not
nominated by Governor.
• Ensures Assembly predominance in bicameral
states.
• Article 171 governs Council composition.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q5. “To uphold and protect the Sovereignty Unity
and Integrity of India” is a provision made in the
(a) Preamble of the Constitution
(b) Directive Principles of State Policy
(c) Fundamental Rights
(d) Fundamental Duties
Answer: (d)
• Article 51A(c) of Fundamental Duties: Uphold
sovereignty, unity, integrity.
• Added by 42nd Amendment (1976).
• Duties serve as moral obligations for citizens.

•

Promote national unity and constitutional
values.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q6. The fundamental object of Panchayati Raj
system is to ensure which among the following?
1. People’s participation in development
2. Political accountability
3. Democratic decentralization
4. Financial mobilization
Select the correct answer using the code given below
(a) 1, 2 and 3 only
(b) 2 and 4 only
(c) 1 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4
Answer: (c)
• 73rd Amendment: aimed at democratic
decentralization and people’s participation in
local governance.
• Article 40 laid the basis for village
panchayats.
• Political accountability exists but is not the
core object.
• Financial mobilization is not the fundamental
objective.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. The ideal of “Welfare State” in the Indian
Constitution is enshrined in its
(a) Preamble
(b) Directive Principles of State Policy
(c) Fundamental Rights
(d) Seventh Schedule
Answer: (b)
• DPSP (Part IV) guides the State to ensure
social and economic welfare.
• Based on principles of equality of
opportunity, economic justice.
• Non-justiciable but fundamental to
governance (Article 37).
• Inspired by Irish Constitution and Gandhian
principles.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q8. There is a Parliamentary System of Government
in India because the
(a) Lok Sabha is elected directly by the people
(b) Parliament can amend the constitution
(c) RajyaSabha cannot be dissolved
(d) Council of Ministers is responsible to the Lok
Sabha
Answer: (d)
• Article 75(3): Council of Ministers is
collectively responsible to Lok Sabha.
• This executive accountability is the essence of
parliamentary system.
• (a), (b), (c) are true statements but do not
define the system.
• Based on Westminster model.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

3. The RajyaSabha cannot discuss the Annual
Financial Statement.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2 only
(c) 2 and 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
Answer: (b)
• Money Bill: RS can only recommend
amendments within 14 days; LS may
accept/reject.
• Demands for Grants: only Lok Sabha votes.
• RS can discuss the Annual Financial
Statement, but cannot vote on demands.
• Reflects financial supremacy of Lok Sabha.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q9. Consider the following Statements regarding the

2. The provisions contained in these Principles

(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 & 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
Answer: (c)
• DPSP aims at social & economic democracy
through rights like livelihood, education,
equality.
• Non-justiciable under Article 37, but
fundamental to governance.
• Provide policy direction for welfare
legislation.
• Taken from Irish Constitution.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q11. When a bill is referred to a joint sitting both the
Houses of the Parliament, has to be passed by
(a) a simple majority of member present and voting
(b) three-fourths majority of member present and
voting
(c) two-thirds majority of the House
(d) absolute majority of the House
Answer: (a)
• Article 108: Joint sitting resolves deadlock;
not applicable to constitutional amendments.
• Decision by simple majority of members
present and voting.
• Speaker of Lok Sabha presides.
• Used to resolve legislative deadlock between
Houses.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

(d) Neither 1 nor 2

•
•

•

Article 53: Executive power vests in the

Cabinet Secretary is ex officio Chairman of

PM heads Council of Ministers, but
